algebraic integers (E707); Theorem 4.1's reduction to algebraic integers and its induction on the degree are left
implicit (E705, E706). Dimitrov's Lemma 2.3 uses that `P₂` is irreducible where it assumes only that `P₂` is not a
square (E709), and his induction needs the base case of degree one (E710). Smyth's Salem survey: the degree bound
in Lemma 1 is not proved (E702), the reciprocal Pisot numbers are `x² − ax + 1` with `a ≥ 3` (E701), and the lattice
point in Proposition 4 must be nonzero (E715). The Euler Circle note writes `x³ − x + 1` for `x³ − x − 1` (E711).

## CA.7 Integral Galois modules and orders

**Coverage: partial.** Integral Galois module structure, planned from Johnston's Notes on Galois Modules (read in full), Ullom 1969, Taylor 1981 (statements), Voight's Quaternion Algebras (orders and locally free lattices), Conrad's two expository articles, Ferri–Greither and Acciaro. The layer's targets are all realised by nodes: group-ring modules for rings of integers, the rational normal basis map, normal integral bases, tame ramification and the trace, local and global Noether theorems, the Hilbert–Speiser theorem with Gauss periods, orders, maximal orders and the associated order, resolvents and the resolvent–discriminant relation, locally free lattices and class groups, the Galois module class, and the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems stated source-scoped. The two acceptance gates are nodes: ℚ(i)/ℚ (a rational normal basis that is not integral, wild) and a nonprincipal ideal (local freeness without freeness). Three recorded gaps keep the layer partial.

**Remaining in this layer:**

- The proofs of Fröhlich's kernel-group theorem and Taylor's Theorem 1 (see the gap on the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems): Fröhlich's Hom-description of Cl(ℤΓ), the root number class t(W), Galois Gauss sums and the group logarithm.
- Jacobinski's cancellation theorem under the Eichler condition, needed for the freeness corollary of Taylor's theorem.
- Reiner's Theorem 27.4 for orders in semisimple algebras (every locally free class is represented by a locally free ideal), public only for simple algebras (Voight Proposition 20.7.4).

This layer develops the additive Galois module structure of rings of integers. For a finite Galois extension L/K of number fields with group G, the ring of integers 𝓞_L is a module over the group ring 𝓞_K[G]. The rational normal basis theorem says that L is free of rank one over K[G]; the integral question is whether 𝓞_L is free of rank one over 𝓞_K[G] (a normal integral basis), locally free (Noether), stably free, or what its class is in the locally free class group Cl(𝓞_K[G]) and in Cl(ℤ[G]) (Fröhlich, Taylor). Tame ramification is the dividing line: a normal integral basis forces it (Speiser), it is equivalent to local freeness (Noether), and over ℚ it is equivalent to a normal integral basis for abelian fields (Hilbert–Speiser). The layer builds the objects this needs and that no library has: the group-ring module structure, normal integral bases, the tame predicate for Dedekind extensions, orders, left orders, maximal orders and associated orders, locally free lattices, locally free class groups and the Galois module class, Lagrange resolvents and resolvends.

**Sources.** Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules* (version of 18 October 2016), read in full, is the primary source; Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields* (Nagoya Math. J. 34, 1969) supplies Rim's criterion and the projectivity form of Noether's theorem; Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions* (Invent. Math. 63, 1981) supplies the statements of the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems; Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, supplies orders, maximal orders, locally free lattices and the stable class group; Conrad's *The origin of representation theory* and *Linear independence of characters* supply the group determinant and the rational normal basis examples; Ferri–Greither (arXiv:1805.12588) and Acciaro (arXiv:1704.00359) supply the kernel group and augmentation map and the discriminant criterion. URLs and SHA-256 hashes are in the packet.

**Pinned conventions.**

- *Setting.* A is a commutative ring, B a commutative A-algebra and G a finite group acting on B by A-algebra automorphisms; the Galois statements assume Mathlib's IsGaloisGroup G A B (faithful action commuting with A whose invariants are A). For number fields, A = 𝓞_K, B = 𝓞_L, G = Gal(L/K) = L ≃ₐ[K] L, and the instance is inferred. For nonarchimedean local fields, A = 𝒪_K is a complete discrete valuation ring with finite residue field and B = 𝒪_L its integral closure in L.
- *Group ring.* A[G] is Mathlib's MonoidAlgebra A G. It acts on B on the left, (Σ a_g g)·x = Σ a_g g(x), through the representation Representation.ofDistribMulAction A G B and its asModule. No right action is used; induced modules are A[G] ⊗_{A[H]} N.
- *Normal integral basis.* A basis of B over A indexed by G whose value at g is g·α, as Mathlib's IsGalois.normalBasis is indexed by the Galois group with normalBasis_apply.
- *Tame.* B/A is tame at a prime P of B over p = P ∩ A when the residue extension is separable and the characteristic of A/p does not divide P.ramificationIdx A (the unprimed Mathlib ramification index, prime first). This is the convention of the NumberFieldArithmetic roadmap; for number fields and local fields residue separability is automatic. The local predicate of the LocalFieldsRamification roadmap is compared through the invariant matching of NumberFieldArithmetic Layer 5.5.
- *Completions.* M_v = Â_v ⊗_A M with Â_v = v.adicCompletionIntegers K for v : HeightOneSpectrum A. Local freeness is stated with completions, as in Johnston Definition 10.2 and Voight 20.2.8.
- *Trace.* Tr_{B/A} is Mathlib's Algebra.intTrace A B; in the Galois case it is Σ_g g.
- *Resolvents.* ⟨a | χ⟩ = Σ_σ χ(σ⁻¹)σ(a) and r_G(a) = Σ_σ σ(a)σ⁻¹ ∈ L[G], the convention of Fröhlich and Taylor.
- *K₀ and class groups.* K₀(Λ) is Tau Ceti's split K₀ of the finitely generated projective Λ-modules (TauCeti.SplitK0 of TauCeti.finiteProjectiveModules Λ, valid for noncommutative Λ); Cl(Λ) is the subgroup generated by [M] − n[Λ] for M locally free of rank n. Cl(ℤΓ) is written additively; Taylor writes it multiplicatively.

**What is already built, and cited rather than planned.** Mathlib has the rational normal basis theorem (IsGalois.normalBasis, normalBasis_apply, exists_linearIndependent_algEquiv_apply, all in the declaration index at the pin), the Galois action on 𝓞_L with IsGaloisGroup, the trace (Algebra.intTrace, trace_eq_sum_automorphisms, Algebra.trace_quotient_eq_of_isDedekindDomain), ramification indices and inertia groups (Ideal.ramificationIdx, ramificationIdxIn, card_inertia_eq_ramificationIdxIn), the cyclotomic ring of integers ℤ[ζ_n] for every n and the ramification index p^k(p − 1) in ℚ(ζ_n), arithmetic disjointness (Module.Basis.ofIsCoprimeDifferentIdeal with the coprime-discriminant lemmas), R-lattices (Submodule.IsLattice), representations and their modules, projective and faithfully flat modules, Nakayama's lemma, the Vandermonde determinant and Dedekind's independence of characters. Tau Ceti has the Teichmüller lift of a local field and split K₀ for any ring. The audit's remark that the normal basis theorem is missing from the index is not borne out at the pin. No library has a tame-ramification predicate, normal integral bases, orders or associated orders in noncommutative algebras, locally free lattices or class groups, or resolvents.

**Imported from other roadmaps.** Kronecker–Weber from ClassFieldTheory Layer 13; unramified extensions, the tame totally ramified structure theorem and its Galois criterion from LocalFieldsRamification Layers 2 and 3; the integral semilocal equivalence, decomposition groups and invariant matching at completions from NumberFieldArithmetic Layer 5; the Frobenius–Schur trichotomy from RepresentationTheory/CharacterTheory Layer 7; ring K₀ with its stable-equality criterion and scalar extension from KTheoryLowDegrees Z.1, and the rank/Pic classification over a Dedekind domain from KTheoryLowDegrees Z.4. Certified algorithms (computing normal integral bases, the Bley–Johnston freeness test) belong to ComputationalNumberTheory CN.2. Leopoldt's theorem on associated orders of abelian fields and McCulloh's description of realisable classes are outside this layer.

**Planets of this layer** (6): *Ring of integers as a Galois module*, *Normal integral basis*, *Noether's theorem*, *Hilbert–Speiser theorem*, *Locally free class group*, *Taylor's theorem (Fröhlich's conjecture)*.

### CA.7.A Galois modules, normal bases, tameness and the trace

The objects of the layer and the elementary theorems relating them. The ring of integers is a module over the group ring; the rational normal basis theorem makes L free of rank one over K[G]; a normal integral basis asks the same of 𝓞_L over 𝓞_K[G]. Tameness is characterised by surjectivity of the trace, a normal integral basis forces surjectivity of the trace (Speiser), and tameness makes 𝓞_L projective over the group ring (Ullom, by Rim's criterion). ℚ(i)/ℚ is the first counterexample gate.

#### `ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module` — The ring of integers as a module over the group ring

*construction.* **Planet:** *Ring of integers as a Galois module*. Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Basic`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let A be a commutative ring, B a commutative A-algebra and G a group acting on B by ring automorphisms that commute with the action of A (G acts by A-algebra automorphisms). The Galois representation of G on B is the A-linear representation g ↦ (x ↦ g·x), and the group ring A[G] (Mathlib's MonoidAlgebra A G) acts on B on the left by (Σ_g a_g g)·x = Σ_g a_g g(x); this makes B a left A[G]-module whose restriction to A is the given A-module structure. The principal case is a finite Galois extension L/K of number fields with G = Gal(L/K), A = 𝓞_K and B = 𝓞_L, where the action is the restriction of the Galois action on L; the same construction with A = K and B = L gives L as a K[G]-module, and the inclusion 𝓞_L ⊆ L is 𝓞_K[G]-linear. When G is a Galois group for B/A in Mathlib's sense (IsGaloisGroup G A B: faithful action, commuting with A, with invariants the image of A), the norm element N = Σ_g g acts on B with values in A, and for rings of integers it acts as the trace.

**Hypotheses.**

- A is a commutative ring and B a commutative A-algebra.
- G is a group acting on B by ring automorphisms commuting with A: [MulSemiringAction G B] and [SMulCommClass G A B].
- The Galois-group hypothesis IsGaloisGroup G A B is needed only for the statements about invariants and the norm element.
- The group ring acts on the left; a right action is never used.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define the representation as Mathlib's Representation.ofDistribMulAction A G B; its value at g is the A-linear map x ↦ g·x.
2. Take the A[G]-module to be the representation's asModule, with the identification toGroupRingModule : B ≃ₗ[A] ρ.asModule; the module structure is Module.compHom along asAlgebraHom.
3. Check the evaluation formula on single g a and the scalar tower A → A[G] → B from the definition of asAlgebraHom as MonoidAlgebra.lift.
4. For invariants use the IsInvariant field of IsGaloisGroup: the fixed points of G in B are the image of A.
5. For rings of integers, the instance IsGaloisGroup Gal(L/K) 𝓞_K 𝓞_L is found by instance inference at the pin, and trace_eq_sum_automorphisms gives Σ_σ σ(x) = Tr_{L/K}(x); restrict to 𝓞_L through Algebra.algebraMap_intTrace.

**Acceptance.**

- The underlying A-module of the group-ring module is the A-module B: the scalar tower A → A[G] → B holds.
- single g 1 acts as g, and the identity of the group ring acts as the identity.
- For number fields the norm element acts as the trace 𝓞_L → 𝓞_K followed by the inclusion; a construction that let G act through a quotient or through a non-faithful action would fail the invariants characterisation.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.galoisRep` | data | The A-linear representation of G on B, g ↦ (x ↦ g·x). |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.galoisRep_apply` | simp | galoisRep A B G g x = g·x. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.GroupRingModule` | constructor | B as a left A[G]-module: the asModule of galoisRep. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.toGroupRingModule` | equivalence | The A-linear identification of B with its group-ring module. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.single_smul` | simp | single g a · x = a · g(x). |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.of_smul` | simp | The image of g in A[G] acts as g. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.isScalarTower` | instance | The scalar tower A → A[G] → B. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.norm_smul` | relation | The norm element Σ_g g acts as x ↦ Σ_g g(x). |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.mem_range_algebraMap_iff` | characterisation | For a Galois group G of B/A, x lies in the image of A exactly when g(x) = x for all g. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.sum_smul_mem_range_algebraMap` | relation | For a Galois group G of B/A, Σ_g g(x) lies in the image of A. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.galoisRep_numberField_apply` | compatibility | For number fields, the value of galoisRep at σ ∈ Gal(L/K) on x ∈ 𝓞_L is σ(x) computed in L. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`: the normal integral basis is a basis of this module over A[G] of rank one
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice`: local freeness of 𝓞_L is local freeness of this A[G]-lattice
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order`: the associated order is the stabiliser of 𝓞_L inside K[G] acting on L through this construction
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-class`: the Galois module class is the class of this module in the locally free class group
- Johnston, Notes on Galois modules, Section 1.3: every statement about normal integral bases is a statement about 𝓞_L as an 𝓞_K[G]-module

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralGaloisModule.galoisRep_test_trivialGroup` (degenerate): For the trivial group the norm element acts as the identity.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.galoisRep_test_augmentationKillsInvariants` (characterisation): For a Galois group of B/A, g − 1 kills the image of A in B.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.galoisRep_test_normIsTrace` (compatibility): For number fields, Σ_σ σ(x) equals the image of Tr_{𝓞_L/𝓞_K}(x) (Mathlib's Algebra.intTrace).

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Representation.ofDistribMulAction`
- `mathlib:Representation.asModule`
- `mathlib:Representation.asAlgebraHom`
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`
- `mathlib:IsGaloisGroup`
- `mathlib:Algebra.IsInvariant`
- `mathlib:galRestrict`
- `mathlib:trace_eq_sum_automorphisms`
- `mathlib:Algebra.intTrace`
- `mathlib:Algebra.algebraMap_intTrace`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 1.1 and Examples 1.3 (printed p. 1).

  > A left G-module consists of an abelian group M on which G acts on the left such that g · (a + b) = g · a + g · b. Alternatively, one may think of M as a left Z[G]-module.

  The node is the left group-ring module structure on the ring of integers, Example 1.3(ii), over the group ring of the base ring of integers.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 1.6 (printed p. 2).

  > we say that L/K has a normal integral basis (NIB) if there exists an element α ∈ O_L such that the Galois conjugates of α form an O_K-basis of O_L, or equivalently, O_L is a free O_K[G]-module of rank 1.

  The definition presupposes exactly the O_K[G]-module structure constructed here.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Chapter I, first paragraph (printed p. 154).

  > The ring of integers O_K is a Dedekind domain and a G-module under Galois action.

  The same structure in the AKLB generality used by the node.

#### `rational-normal-basis-map` — The rational normal basis map K[G] ≅ L

*construction.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/NormalBasis`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.NormalBasis`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of fields with group G. An element θ of L is a normal basis generator if its conjugates (σθ)_{σ∈G} are K-linearly independent, equivalently span L, equivalently form a K-basis of L. For a generator θ the map K[G] → L, x ↦ x·θ, is an isomorphism of left K[G]-modules, sending single σ c to c·σ(θ). Mathlib's IsGalois.normalBasis supplies the canonical generator θ₀ = normalBasis K L 1, and the normal basis map of L/K is the map attached to θ₀. For number fields, A = 𝓞_K and B = 𝓞_L, the map carries the group ring 𝓞_K[G] onto the 𝓞_K-lattice 𝓞_K[G]·θ₀ of L, which in general is not 𝓞_L.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K is a finite Galois extension of fields: [FiniteDimensional K L] [IsGalois K L].
- The group ring acts on L through the Galois representation of the construction ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module with A = K and B = L.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Linear independence of the conjugates is IsGenerator; since the index set G has cardinality [L : K] (IsGalois.card_aut_eq_finrank), independence is equivalent to spanning and to being a basis (basisOfLinearIndependentOfCardEqFinrank).
2. Mathlib's IsGalois.normalBasis is a basis indexed by G with normalBasis_apply: normalBasis σ = σ(normalBasis 1); hence normalBasis 1 is a generator.
3. Define equivOfGenerator θ as the composite of the K-linear equivalence K[G] ≃ (G →₀ K) with the representation isomorphism of the basis σ ↦ σθ.
4. K[G]-linearity: for σ, τ ∈ G, (single σ 1)·(τθ) = (στ)θ, which is the image of single σ 1 * single τ 1; extend by linearity.

**Acceptance.**

- equiv K L (single σ 1) = σ(normalBasis K L 1).
- In a nontrivial extension 1 is never a generator, and over the trivial group every nonzero element is one.
- For ℚ(i)/ℚ the element 1 + i is a generator, since its conjugates 1 + i and 1 − i are linearly independent.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `NormalBasis.IsGenerator` | data | θ is a normal basis generator: its conjugates are K-linearly independent. |
| `NormalBasis.isGenerator_normalBasis_one` | constructor | Mathlib's normal basis generator normalBasis K L 1 is a generator. |
| `NormalBasis.isGenerator_iff_span` | characterisation | θ is a generator exactly when its conjugates span L. |
| `NormalBasis.basisOfGenerator` | constructor | The K-basis (σθ)_σ of L defined by a generator. |
| `NormalBasis.basisOfGenerator_apply` | simp | basisOfGenerator h σ = σθ. |
| `NormalBasis.equivOfGenerator` | equivalence | The isomorphism K[G] ≃ L, x ↦ x·θ, for a generator θ. |
| `NormalBasis.equivOfGenerator_single` | simp | single σ c ↦ c·σθ. |
| `NormalBasis.equivOfGenerator_mul` | compatibility | equivOfGenerator (x * y) = x · equivOfGenerator y: the map is K[G]-linear. |
| `NormalBasis.equiv` | data | The normal basis map attached to Mathlib's normal basis. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-basis-generators-differ-by-units`: two generators differ by a unit of K[G], computed through this map
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order`: the associated order is defined inside K[G], identified with L by this map
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/lagrange-resolvent`: a normal basis generator is detected by its resolvents
- Johnston, Remark 3.14(iii): via this isomorphism 𝓞_L is identified with an 𝓞_K-lattice in K[G]

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `NormalBasis.test_trivialGroup` (degenerate): Over the trivial group an element is a generator exactly when it is nonzero.
- `NormalBasis.test_one_not_generator` (non-example): In a nontrivial Galois extension 1 is not a generator: its conjugates are all equal.
- `NormalBasis.test_quadratic_generator` (computation): In a quadratic extension with nontrivial automorphism σ and σx = −x ≠ 0, the element 1 + x is a generator, as 1 + i is for ℚ(i)/ℚ.
- `NormalBasis.test_equiv_single_one` (compatibility): The normal basis map sends single σ 1 to σ(normalBasis K L 1), agreeing with Mathlib's normalBasis_apply.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:IsGalois.normalBasis`
- `mathlib:IsGalois.normalBasis_apply`
- `mathlib:exists_linearIndependent_algEquiv_apply`
- `mathlib:IsGalois.card_aut_eq_finrank`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Theorem 1.4 (printed p. 1).

  > Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of fields with Galois group G. Then there exists an element α ∈ L such that the Galois conjugates of α form a K-basis for L. In other words, L = K[G] · α and so L is a free K[G]-module of rank 1.

  The node packages the existence statement, which is Mathlib's IsGalois.normalBasis, as the K[G]-module isomorphism the theorem asserts.

- conrad-linear-characters-2026 — Keith Conrad, *Linear independence of characters*, Section 3, Example 3.1 (p. 2).

  > The set {1 + i, 1 − i} is a normal basis: its elements are R-conjugate and they are linearly independent over R.

  The quadratic test 1 + x with σx = −x is this example; Mathlib's normal basis file cites this handout for its proof.

#### `normal-basis-generators-differ-by-units` — Normal basis generators differ by units of the group algebra

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/NormalBasis`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.NormalBasis`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be finite Galois with group G and let θ be a normal basis generator. An element β of L is a normal basis generator if and only if β = u·θ for a unit u of K[G]; the unit is unique.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois; θ a normal basis generator.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. By the normal basis map, β = u·θ for a unique u ∈ K[G].
2. β is a generator exactly when x ↦ x·β = (x u)·θ is bijective, that is when right multiplication by u on K[G] is bijective.
3. K[G] is finite dimensional over K, so right multiplication by u is bijective exactly when it is injective, exactly when u is a unit.

**Acceptance.**

- For θ a generator and c ∈ K nonzero, cθ is a generator (u = c).
- For G nontrivial, (Σ_g g)·θ is not a generator: Σ_g g is a zero divisor in K[G].

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/rational-normal-basis-map`
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Remark 1.5 (printed p. 2).

  > Suppose α ∈ L such that L = K[G] · α and let β ∈ L. Then L = K[G] · β if and only if there exists u ∈ (K[G])^× such that β = u · α.

  Verbatim the node; uniqueness of u is the injectivity of the normal basis map.

#### `normal-integral-basis` — Normal integral bases

*definition.* **Planet:** *Normal integral basis*. Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/NormalIntegralBasis`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let A be a commutative ring, B a commutative A-algebra and G a finite Galois group of B/A (IsGaloisGroup G A B). An element α of B generates a normal integral basis if its conjugates (g·α)_{g∈G} form an A-basis of B: they are A-linearly independent and span B. Equivalently the A[G]-linear map A[G] → B, x ↦ x·α, is bijective, so B is free of rank one over A[G] with basis α. B has a normal integral basis over A if some α generates one. For a finite Galois extension of number fields L/K the relevant case is A = 𝓞_K, B = 𝓞_L, G = Gal(L/K); for a finite Galois extension of nonarchimedean local fields it is A = 𝒪_K, B = 𝒪_L.

**Hypotheses.**

- G is finite and is a Galois group of B/A in Mathlib's sense.
- The basis is indexed by G itself, as in Mathlib's IsGalois.normalBasis, not by an arbitrary finite type.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define IsNIBGenerator α as the conjunction of LinearIndependent A (g ↦ g·α) and span = ⊤, and HasNIB as the existence of such α.
2. Build the basis with Module.Basis.mk and record basis_apply: basis g = g·α.
3. The bijectivity characterisation: x ↦ x·α sends single g a to a·gα, so bijectivity is exactly the basis property of (gα)_g.
4. Change of generator: if α and β generate, the A[G]-linear automorphism of A[G] carrying 1 to the coordinate vector of β is right multiplication by a unit.
5. Compatibility with the field: for number fields an 𝓞_K-basis of 𝓞_L is a K-basis of L (𝓞_L is a lattice spanning L), so a normal integral basis generator is a normal basis generator of L/K.

**Acceptance.**

- ζ_p generates a normal integral basis of ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ for an odd prime p.
- 1 never generates a normal integral basis of a nontrivial extension.
- ℚ(i)/ℚ has none, although 1 + i generates a rational normal basis.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsNIBGenerator` | data | α generates a normal integral basis: (gα)_g is an A-basis of B. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.HasNIB` | data | B has a normal integral basis over A. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsNIBGenerator.basis` | constructor | The A-basis of B indexed by G defined by a generator. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsNIBGenerator.basis_apply` | simp | basis g = g·α. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.isNIBGenerator_iff_bijective` | characterisation | α generates exactly when x ↦ x·α, A[G] → B, is bijective (B free of rank one over A[G]). |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsNIBGenerator.unit_smul` | relation | u·α generates for every unit u of A[G]. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsNIBGenerator.exists_unit` | relation | Two generators differ by a unit of A[G]. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsNIBGenerator.isGenerator` | compatibility | For number fields a normal integral basis generator is a normal basis generator of L/K. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-of-normal-integral-basis`: Speiser: an extension with a normal integral basis is tame
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-noether`: Noether: a local extension has a normal integral basis exactly when it is tame
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/hilbert-speiser`: Hilbert–Speiser: an abelian number field has a normal integral basis exactly when it is tame
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/gaussian-integers-have-no-normal-integral-basis`: the counterexample gate is a negative statement about this predicate
- Taylor 1981, Section 2: Noether gives a ∈ 𝓞_M with a·𝓞_{N,l}Γ = 𝓞_{M,l}, a local normal integral basis at each l

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralGaloisModule.nib_test_trivialGroup` (degenerate): Over the trivial group, α generates exactly when it is a unit of B = A.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.nib_test_one_not_generator` (non-example): For a nontrivial group 1 does not generate: its conjugates coincide.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.nib_test_cyclotomicPrime` (computation): For an odd prime p, ζ_p generates a normal integral basis of ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/rational-normal-basis-map`
- `mathlib:IsGaloisGroup`
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`
- `mathlib:Module.Free`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 1.6 (printed p. 2).

  > we say that L/K has a normal integral basis (NIB) if there exists an element α ∈ O_L such that the Galois conjugates of α form an O_K-basis of O_L, or equivalently, O_L is a free O_K[G]-module of rank 1.

  Verbatim the definition, stated for any Galois group of B/A so that the number-field and local cases are the same declaration.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Remark 3.14(ii) (printed p. 6).

  > If O_L is free over A_{L/K}, then it must be free of rank 1 and so there exists α ∈ O_L such that O_L = A_{L/K} · α. Such an α always generates a normal basis for L/K, i.e. L = K[G] · α.

  The compatibility item: a normal integral basis generator is a rational normal basis generator.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Definition following Corollary 1.4 (printed p. 156).

  > An ideal 𝔄 of K has an O_F G-normal basis if 𝔄 is isomorphic to the group ring O_F G (isomorphism of O_F G-modules) equivalently ∃ α ∈ 𝔄 such that every element of 𝔄 may be put in the form Σ a_σ α^σ, a_σ ∈ O_F.

  The same definition for the ring of integers.

#### `trace-of-normal-integral-basis-generator` — The trace of a normal integral basis generator generates for a Galois subextension

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/NormalIntegralBasis`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of number fields with group G, let F be an intermediate field with F/K Galois, H = Gal(L/F) and Γ = Gal(F/K) ≅ G/H. If α ∈ 𝓞_L generates a normal integral basis of L/K then Tr_{L/F}(α) ∈ 𝓞_F generates a normal integral basis of F/K.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K and F/K finite Galois, K ⊆ F ⊆ L number fields.
- The trace is Mathlib's Algebra.intTrace 𝓞_F 𝓞_L.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Write x ∈ 𝓞_F as x = Σ_g c(g) g(α) with unique c(g) ∈ 𝓞_K.
2. Since h(x) = x for h ∈ H and the coefficients are unique, c(h⁻¹g) = c(g) for h ∈ H.
3. Choose lifts σ̃ of σ ∈ Γ; regroup the sum as Σ_σ c(σ̃) Σ_{h∈H} hσ̃(α) = Σ_σ c(σ̃) σ(Tr_{L/F}(α)), using that H is normal.
4. Linear independence of (σ(Tr_{L/F} α))_σ over 𝓞_K follows from that of (gα)_g, since the lifts times H partition G.

**Acceptance.**

- For F = K the conclusion is that Tr_{L/K}(α) generates 𝓞_K over 𝓞_K, i.e. is a unit.
- For L = ℚ(ζ_n), n odd squarefree, and F ⊆ L, the conclusion is Proposition 6.6: the Gauss period Tr(ζ_n) generates.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module`
- `mathlib:Algebra.intTrace`
- `mathlib:trace_eq_sum_automorphisms`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proposition 6.1 with its proof (printed pp. 10–11).

  > Let F be an intermediate field between L and K with F/K Galois. If α generates a normal integral basis for L/K, then Tr_{L/F}(α) generates a normal integral basis for F/K.

  Verbatim the node.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Proposition 1.6 (printed p. 156).

  > If 𝔄 = O_F G [α], then 𝔄 ∩ L = O_F(G/H) [S_{K/L}(α)].

  The same statement for ambiguous ideals; the ring of integers is the case 𝔄 = O_K.

- conrad-linear-characters-2026 — Keith Conrad, *Linear independence of characters*, Theorem 3.8 (p. 5).

  > If N ◁ G then a normal basis for K(α_N)/K is {τ(α_N) : τ ∈ G/N}.

  The rational analogue, whose proof is the same regrouping.

#### `trace-surjective-of-normal-integral-basis` — A normal integral basis forces the trace to be surjective

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/NormalIntegralBasis`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of number fields with a normal integral basis, and F an intermediate field. Then Tr_{L/F}(𝓞_L) = 𝓞_F; in particular Tr_{L/K}(𝓞_L) = 𝓞_K. In the Galois-group form: if B has a normal integral basis over A for the Galois group G, then Tr_{B/A} : B → A is surjective.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois; F any intermediate field (F/K need not be Galois: the regrouping runs over the cosets of Gal(L/F) in G).

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Tr_{L/F}(𝓞_L) ⊆ 𝓞_F always.
2. For x ∈ 𝓞_F, the regrouping of the previous lemma writes x = Tr_{L/F}(Σ_σ c(σ̃)σ̃(α)) with Σ_σ c(σ̃)σ̃(α) ∈ 𝓞_L.
3. In the Galois-group form, Tr_{B/A}(a·α) over the basis: Tr(Σ_g c_g gα) = (Σ_g c_g)·Tr(α), and Tr(α) is a unit by the previous lemma with F = K, so the trace is onto.

**Acceptance.**

- For ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ, Tr(ζ_p) = −1 is a unit.
- For ℚ(i)/ℚ, Tr(ℤ[i]) = 2ℤ, so ℚ(i)/ℚ has no normal integral basis.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/trace-of-normal-integral-basis-generator`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`
- `mathlib:Algebra.intTrace`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Corollary 6.2 (printed p. 11).

  > If L/K has a normal integral basis, then Tr_{L/F}(O_L) = O_F. In particular, Tr_{L/K}(O_L) = O_K.

  Verbatim the node.

#### `tamely-ramified` — Tamely ramified extensions of Dedekind domains

*definition.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Tame`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let B/A be an extension of commutative rings and P a prime of B lying over p = P ∩ A. B/A is tamely ramified at P if the residue extension (B/P)/(A/p) is separable and the characteristic of A/p does not divide the ramification index e(P|p) (Mathlib's P.ramificationIdx A). B/A is tamely ramified if it is tamely ramified at every nonzero prime of B. When the residue fields are finite (number fields, nonarchimedean local fields) the separability condition holds automatically and the condition is p ∤ e(P|p) for every P, which is the definition of the sources; the separability clause is the convention of Tau Ceti's NumberFieldArithmetic roadmap, so that the predicate is correct in every characteristic.

**Hypotheses.**

- The ramification index is the unprimed Mathlib P.ramificationIdx A (prime P of B first, base ring A second).
- The base prime is P.under A with the LiesOver instance Ideal.over_under.
- The residue characteristic is ringChar (A ⧸ P.under A).

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define IsTamelyRamifiedAt P as separability of the residue algebra (Ideal.Quotient.algebraOfLiesOver) together with non-divisibility of the ramification index.
2. Define IsTamelyRamified as tameness at every nonzero prime of B.
3. Finite residue fields: a finite field is perfect, so every finite extension of it is separable; this gives the simplified form.
4. Towers: for A ⊆ B ⊆ C Dedekind, ramification indices multiply (Mathlib's ramificationIdxIn_mul_ramificationIdxIn in the Galois case, and the multiplicativity of e in general), residue separability is transitive, and every prime of B lies under a prime of C; so C/A is tame exactly when C/B and B/A are.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ is tame for an odd prime p: the only ramified prime is p with e = p − 1.
- ℚ(i)/ℚ is not tame: 2 ramifies with e = 2.
- An unramified extension (e = 1 everywhere) is tame.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsTamelyRamifiedAt` | data | Tameness at a prime P of B: separable residue extension and residue characteristic not dividing e(P|p). |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.IsTamelyRamified` | data | Tameness at every nonzero prime of B. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.isTamelyRamifiedAt_iff_of_finite` | characterisation | With finite residue fields, tameness at P is p ∤ e(P|p). |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.isTamelyRamifiedAt_of_ramificationIdx_eq_one` | constructor | An unramified prime with separable residue extension is tame. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.isTamelyRamified_trans_iff` | functoriality | In a tower A ⊆ B ⊆ C of Dedekind domains, C/A is tame exactly when B/A and C/B are. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.isTamelyRamified_numberField_iff` | compatibility | For number fields, tameness is p ∤ e(P|p) at every nonzero prime P of 𝓞_L, the convention of the NumberFieldArithmetic roadmap. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-iff-trace-surjective`: tameness is characterised by surjectivity of the trace
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/noether-theorem`: tameness is the condition in Noether's theorem
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/hilbert-speiser`: tameness is the condition in the Hilbert–Speiser theorem
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-noether`: local tameness gives a local normal integral basis
- Taylor 1981, Introduction: the theorem is stated for tame Galois extensions M/N

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralGaloisModule.tame_test_self` (degenerate): The trivial extension A/A of a Dedekind domain is tamely ramified.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.tame_test_cyclotomicPrime` (computation): ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ is tamely ramified for an odd prime p.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.tame_test_gaussian_not_tame` (non-example): ℚ(i)/ℚ is not tamely ramified: e = 2 at the prime above 2.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Ideal.ramificationIdx`
- `mathlib:Algebra.IsSeparable`
- `mathlib:Ideal.LiesOver`
- `mathlib:Ideal.ramificationIdxIn_mul_ramificationIdxIn`
- `mathlib:IsDedekindDomain`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 7.1 (printed p. 13).

  > Let p be the residue characteristic of O_K/p. We say that P_i is wildly ramified in L/K if p | e_i; otherwise we say P_i is tamely ramified in L/K (this includes the case e_i = 1).

  The node is this definition, with the residue-separability clause made explicit.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Definition, Chapter I (printed p. 154).

  > Let 𝔓 be a prime ideal of K with ramification index e over F. 𝔓 is tamely ramified over F if the characteristic of the field O_K/𝔓 ∩ O_F is prime to e.

  The same definition; Ullom assumes separable residue extensions throughout, which is the separability clause.

#### `trace-modulo-a-prime` — The trace modulo a prime in a Galois extension

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Tame`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let B/A be a finite extension of Dedekind domains with a finite Galois group G (IsGaloisGroup G A B), p a nonzero prime of A with pB = (P_1⋯P_g)^e, and x ∈ B. In A/p, Tr_{(B/pB)/(A/p)}(x mod pB) = e·Σ_{i=1}^{g} Tr_{(B/P_i)/(A/p)}(x mod P_i); and by Mathlib this is (Tr_{B/A} x) mod p.

**Hypotheses.**

- p nonzero maximal; e = p.ramificationIdxIn B, the common ramification index of the primes over p in the Galois case.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. The Chinese remainder theorem identifies B/pB with Π_i B/P_i^e.
2. Each B/P_i^e has the filtration by P_i^j/P_i^e whose successive quotients P_i^j/P_i^{j+1} are one-dimensional B/P_i-spaces; in a basis adapted to the filtration, multiplication by x is block upper triangular with e diagonal blocks equal to multiplication by x mod P_i on B/P_i.
3. The trace of a block triangular matrix is the sum of the traces of the diagonal blocks.
4. Mathlib's Algebra.trace_quotient_eq_of_isDedekindDomain identifies the left side with the reduction of Algebra.intTrace A B x.

**Acceptance.**

- For p unramified (e = 1) the formula is the sum of the residue traces.
- For ℤ[i] and p = 2 (e = 2, g = 1) both sides vanish identically in 𝔽_2.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Algebra.trace_quotient_eq_of_isDedekindDomain`
- `mathlib:Ideal.ramificationIdxIn`
- `mathlib:Algebra.intTrace`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tamely-ramified`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Proposition 7.2, equation (5) (printed p. 13).

  > With respect to ∪_i B_i, the matrix of m_α is block upper-diagonal, where each of the eg blocks correponds to the endomorphism m_{α_i} on L_i ≅ P_i^{j−1}/P_i^j ... Therefore in K we have (5) Tr_{L/K}(α) = e Σ_{i=1}^g Tr_{L_i/K}(α_i).

  The node is equation (5) with its proof.

#### `prime-divides-trace-ideal-iff-not-tame` — A prime divides the trace ideal exactly when it is not tame

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Tame`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let B/A be a finite extension of Dedekind domains with a finite Galois group G, and p a nonzero prime of A. Then Tr_{B/A}(B) ⊆ p if and only if some prime P of B over p is not tamely ramified.

**Hypotheses.**

- Galois case: all primes over p have the same ramification index.
- Tr_{B/A}(B) is an ideal of A.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. If some P over p is not tame: either the residue characteristic divides e, and then equation (5) is e·(…) = 0 in A/p for every x; or some residue extension is inseparable, and then all residue extensions are (they are conjugate under G) and every residue trace vanishes (Mathlib's Algebra.trace_eq_zero_of_not_isSeparable). Either way Tr(x) ∈ p for all x.
2. If every P over p is tame: the residue extension of P_1 is separable, so its trace is nonzero (Algebra.trace_ne_zero); choose β_1 with nonzero residue trace, and by the Chinese remainder theorem β ≡ β_1 mod P_1, β ≡ 0 mod P_i (i ≠ 1). Then Tr(β) ≡ e·Tr(β_1) ≠ 0 mod p since p ∤ e.

**Acceptance.**

- For ℤ[i]/ℤ and p = 2: Tr(ℤ[i]) = 2ℤ ⊆ (2).
- For ℤ[ζ_p]/ℤ, p odd: Tr(ℤ[ζ_p]) = ℤ is contained in no prime.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/trace-modulo-a-prime`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tamely-ramified`
- `mathlib:Algebra.trace_eq_zero_of_not_isSeparable`
- `mathlib:Algebra.trace_ne_zero`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proposition 7.2 (printed p. 13).

  > Let L/K be a finite extension of number fields or p-adic fields. Then Tr_{L/K}(O_L) is an ideal of O_K. Suppose further that L/K is Galois and let p be a maximal ideal of O_K. Then p | Tr_{L/K}(O_L) if and only if p is wildly ramified in L/K.

  The node, with "wildly ramified" read as "not tame", which includes the residue-separability clause of the definition node.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Corollary 1.2 (printed p. 155).

  > If K/F is tamely ramified, then S𝔄 = 𝔄 ∩ F.

  For 𝔄 = O_K this is the tame direction: the trace of O_K is O_F.

#### `tame-iff-trace-surjective` — Tame ramification is surjectivity of the trace

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Tame`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let B/A be a finite extension of Dedekind domains with a finite Galois group G. Then B/A is tamely ramified if and only if Tr_{B/A} : B → A is surjective. For a finite Galois extension of number fields L/K: L/K is tame if and only if Tr_{L/K}(𝓞_L) = 𝓞_K.

**Hypotheses.**

- Galois case, as in the previous theorem.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. The trace ideal is all of A exactly when it lies in no maximal ideal.
2. Apply the previous theorem at every nonzero prime p of A; every nonzero prime of B lies over a nonzero prime of A.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ: trace surjective and tame.
- ℚ(i)/ℚ: trace image 2ℤ and not tame.
- The trace of an element equals Σ_g g(x) (the norm element of the group ring), which is the form used by the projectivity theorems.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/prime-divides-trace-ideal-iff-not-tame`
- `mathlib:Algebra.intTrace`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Corollary 7.3 (printed p. 13).

  > If L/K is a finite Galois extension of number fields or p-adic fields, then L/K is tamely ramified if and only if Tr_{L/K}(O_L) = O_K.

  Verbatim the node.

#### `tame-of-normal-integral-basis` — Speiser: a normal integral basis forces tame ramification

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Tame`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** If B/A (a finite extension of Dedekind domains with finite Galois group G) has a normal integral basis, then B/A is tamely ramified. In particular a finite Galois extension of number fields or of nonarchimedean local fields with a normal integral basis is tamely ramified.

**Hypotheses.**

- Galois case.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. A normal integral basis makes the trace surjective (trace-surjective-of-normal-integral-basis).
2. Surjectivity of the trace is tameness (tame-iff-trace-surjective).

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(i)/ℚ is wild, so has no normal integral basis.
- Every normal integral basis generator found in the Hilbert–Speiser theorem lies in a tame field.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/trace-surjective-of-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-iff-trace-surjective`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Corollary 7.4 (printed p. 14).

  > Let L/K is a finite Galois extension of number fields or p-adic fields. If L/K has a NIB then L/K must be tamely ramified.

  Verbatim the node (the source has a slip of grammar, "Let L/K is").

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Introduction (printed p. 153).

  > A necessary condition that O_K have a normal basis was given by Speiser [9], namely that K/F be tamely ramified.

  The attribution to Speiser.

#### `gaussian-integers-have-no-normal-integral-basis` — ℚ(i)/ℚ: a rational normal basis that is not integral

*application.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Examples`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** For the Gaussian field L = ℚ(i) with G = Gal(L/ℚ) = {1, σ}: 1 + i generates a normal basis of L/ℚ, but L/ℚ has no normal integral basis. Concretely, for α = a + bi ∈ ℤ[i] the conjugates a + bi, a − bi have change-of-basis determinant −2ab relative to {1, i}, never ±1; equivalently Tr(ℤ[i]) = 2ℤ ≠ ℤ, and 2 is wildly ramified.

**Hypotheses.**

- L is the cyclotomic field ℚ(ζ_4) = ℚ(i).

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. 1 + i: the conjugates 1 + i, 1 − i are linearly independent (determinant −2i ≠ 0).
2. No normal integral basis: ℚ(i)/ℚ is not tame (tamely-ramified, test tame_test_gaussian_not_tame), so Speiser applies; or directly, Tr(a + bi) = 2a.

**Acceptance.**

- The acceptance gate of the layer: a rational normal basis need not be integral, and wild ramification is the obstruction.
- The associated order of ℚ(i)/ℚ is strictly larger than ℤ[G] (associated-order-of-gaussian-integers).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-of-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/rational-normal-basis-map`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tamely-ramified`
- `mathlib:IsCyclotomicExtension.Rat.ramificationIdx_eq`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Example 3.16 (printed p. 6).

  > Consider the extension Q(i)/Q. This is wildly ramified at 2 and has Galois group G = {1, σ}, where σ is the restriction of complex conjugation. The ring of integers of Q(i) is Z[i].

  The example the node formalises.

- conrad-linear-characters-2026 — Keith Conrad, *Linear independence of characters*, Example 3.1 (p. 2).

  > The set {1 + i, 1 − i} is a normal basis: its elements are R-conjugate and they are linearly independent over R.

  The rational half of the gate.

#### `rim-projectivity-criterion` — Rim's criterion for projectivity over a group ring

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Projective`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let R be a commutative ring, G a finite group and M a left R[G]-module that is projective as an R-module. If there is an R-linear endomorphism φ of M with Σ_{g∈G} g·φ(g⁻¹·m) = m for all m ∈ M, then M is projective as an R[G]-module.

**Hypotheses.**

- G finite; M projective over R; the scalar tower R → R[G] → M.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. The induced module R[G] ⊗_R M (R[G] acting on the left factor) is R[G]-projective, being the base change of the projective R-module M.
2. The multiplication map μ : R[G] ⊗_R M → M, x ⊗ m ↦ x·m, is R[G]-linear and surjective.
3. s(m) = Σ_g g ⊗ φ(g⁻¹m) is R[G]-linear (reindex the sum) and μ ∘ s = id by the hypothesis.
4. So M is a direct summand of a projective R[G]-module (Module.Projective.of_split).

**Acceptance.**

- If |G| is a unit in R, φ = |G|⁻¹·id works, so every R-projective module is R[G]-projective (Maschke).
- For M = 𝓞_L and φ = multiplication by c with Tr(c) = 1 the hypothesis is Σ_g g(c) = 1.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Module.Projective`
- `mathlib:Module.Projective.of_split`
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`

**Sources.**

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Proof of Proposition 1.3 (printed p. 156).

  > By Rim [8, Prop. 2.3, p. 702] it is enough to show 𝔄 is O_F-projective and there exists an O_F-endomorphism ρ: 𝔄 → 𝔄 such that Σ σρ(σ⁻¹a) = a for all a ∈ 𝔄.

  The criterion as Ullom quotes it; the proof steps are the standard splitting of the induced module.

#### `projective-of-tame` — Ullom: tame ramification makes the integers projective over the group ring

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Projective`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let B/A be a finite extension of Dedekind domains with finite Galois group G. If B/A is tamely ramified then B is a projective A[G]-module (and projective over A[H] for every subgroup H). In particular, for a tame finite Galois extension of number fields, 𝓞_L is a finitely generated projective 𝓞_K[G]-module.

**Hypotheses.**

- Galois case; tame.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. B is finitely generated and torsion free over the Dedekind domain A, hence A-projective.
2. Tameness gives c ∈ B with Tr_{B/A}(c) = 1 (tame-iff-trace-surjective); the trace is Σ_g g (ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module).
3. Take φ = multiplication by c: Σ_g g(c·g⁻¹(m)) = m·Σ_g g(c) = m.
4. Apply Rim's criterion.

**Acceptance.**

- The class of 𝓞_L in K₀(𝓞_K[G]) is defined for every tame L/K.
- For ℚ(i)/ℚ the module ℤ[i] is not ℤ[G]-projective (the converse direction, trace-surjective-of-projective).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/rim-projectivity-criterion`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-iff-trace-surjective`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module`

**Sources.**

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Proposition 1.3 with its proof (printed p. 156).

  > Suppose G = G(K/F) and H is a subgroup of G. K/F tamely ramified implies any ambiguous ideal 𝔄 of K is O_F H-projective.

  The node for 𝔄 = O_K and H = G; the proof takes ρ to be multiplication by β with S_{K/L}(β) = 1.

#### `trace-surjective-of-projective` — Projectivity over the group ring forces the trace to be surjective

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Projective`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let B/A be a finite extension of Dedekind domains with finite Galois group G. If B is projective as an A[G]-module then Tr_{B/A}(B) = A; hence B/A is tamely ramified.

**Hypotheses.**

- Galois case.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. For the free module A[G], the invariants are A·N with N = Σ_g g, and N·A[G] = A·N: the invariants equal the image of the norm.
2. The equality of invariants and norm image passes to direct sums and direct summands, so it holds for every projective A[G]-module.
3. For B: the invariants are A (IsGaloisGroup) and the image of the norm is Tr(B); so Tr(B) = A.
4. Conclude tameness by tame-iff-trace-surjective.

**Acceptance.**

- ℤ[i] is not projective over ℤ[C₂].
- Together with projective-of-tame this is the projectivity form of Noether's theorem.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-iff-trace-surjective`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module`
- `mathlib:Module.Projective`
- `mathlib:Algebra.IsInvariant`

**Sources.**

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Corollary 1.4 (printed p. 156).

  > 𝔄 is cohomologically trivial as a G-module.

  Cohomological triviality of projective modules includes the vanishing of Ĥ⁰(G, B) = B^G/N·B, which is the node.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Proposition 9.9 (printed p. 19).

  > Suppose that L/K is wildly ramified. Let π_K be a prime element of K. Then by Proposition 7.2, (π_K) divides the O_K-ideal Tr_{L/K}(O_L).

  The wild case is detected by the trace ideal, which is what the node's last step uses.

---

### CA.7.B Noether's theorem, local and global

Over a complete discrete valuation ring with finite residue field, a finite Galois extension has a normal integral basis exactly when it is tame (Noether). The proof passes through the unramified case (Nakayama), the totally and tamely ramified case (an explicit Vandermonde computation), and the split extension that combines them, and descends by the trace. Globally, the completion of 𝓞_L at a prime of 𝓞_K is induced from a decomposition group, so a tame extension of number fields has 𝓞_L locally free of rank one over 𝓞_K[G], and conversely. Local freeness is not freeness; a nonprincipal ideal is the second counterexample gate.

#### `local-unramified-normal-integral-basis` — Unramified local extensions have a normal integral basis

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Local`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite unramified Galois extension of nonarchimedean local fields with group G. Then 𝒪_L has a normal integral basis over 𝒪_K; more precisely, any lift α ∈ 𝒪_L of a normal basis generator of the residue extension l/k generates one.

**Hypotheses.**

- A is a complete discrete valuation ring with finite residue field k of cardinality q and characteristic p (the ring of integers of a nonarchimedean local field K, in the vocabulary of the LocalFieldsRamification roadmap); K = Frac A.
- L/K is a finite Galois extension with group G and B is the integral closure of A in L (the valuation ring of L); IsGaloisGroup G A B.
- L/K unramified: e = 1.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Unramified Galois: reduction identifies G with Gal(l/k) (LocalFieldsRamification Layer 2, residue correspondence), and p𝒪_L = 𝔓 is the maximal ideal of 𝒪_L.
2. l/k is a finite Galois extension of finite fields, so it has a normal basis generator ᾱ (Mathlib's normal basis theorem in the finite case).
3. Let α lift ᾱ and M = 𝒪_K[G]·α ⊆ 𝒪_L. Then M/pM → 𝒪_L/p𝒪_L = l is onto, since its image is k[G]·ᾱ = l.
4. Hence 𝒪_L = M + p𝒪_L and Nakayama's lemma gives 𝒪_L = M; comparing ranks ([L : K] = |G|), (gα)_g is an 𝒪_K-basis.

**Acceptance.**

- For K = ℚ_p and L the unramified extension of degree f, a lift of a normal basis generator of 𝔽_{p^f}/𝔽_p generates.
- The trivial extension: α = 1.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:exists_linearIndependent_algEquiv_apply_of_finite`
- `mathlib:Submodule.le_of_le_smul_of_le_jacobson_bot`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-2-unramified-extensions-and-frobenius` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proposition 9.1 with its proof (printed p. 15).

  > Let L/K be a finite unramified extension of p-adic fields. Then L/K has a normal integral basis.

  Verbatim the node; the proof is by Nakayama from a residue normal basis, as in the steps.

#### `local-totally-tamely-ramified-normal-integral-basis` — Totally and tamely ramified local extensions have a normal integral basis

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Local`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a totally and tamely ramified Galois extension of nonarchimedean local fields of degree e, with a uniformiser π_L of L satisfying π_L^e = π_K, a uniformiser of K. Then for any units u_0, …, u_{e−1} of 𝒪_K, α = Σ_{j=0}^{e−1} u_j π_L^j generates a normal integral basis of L/K.

**Hypotheses.**

- A is a complete discrete valuation ring with finite residue field k of cardinality q and characteristic p (the ring of integers of a nonarchimedean local field K, in the vocabulary of the LocalFieldsRamification roadmap); K = Frac A.
- L/K is a finite Galois extension with group G and B is the integral closure of A in L (the valuation ring of L); IsGaloisGroup G A B.
- L/K totally ramified of degree e = [L : K] with p ∤ e; π_L^e = π_K.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. L/K is a cyclic Kummer extension: K contains a primitive e-th root of unity ζ and a generator σ of G acts by σ(π_L) = ζπ_L (LocalFieldsRamification Layer 3, tame totally ramified extensions and their Galois criterion).
2. 𝒪_L = 𝒪_K[π_L] with basis 1, π_L, …, π_L^{e−1} (totally ramified: Eisenstein power basis, LocalFieldsRamification Layer 3).
3. σ^i(α) = Σ_j u_j ζ^{ij} π_L^j, so the change-of-basis matrix is (u_j ζ^{ij}) with determinant (Π u_k)·det(ζ^{ij}).
4. det(ζ^{ij}) is a Vandermonde determinant Π_{i<j}(ζ^j − ζ^i) = ζ^m Π_{i<j}(ζ^{j−i} − 1) (Matrix.det_vandermonde).
5. Each 1 − ζ^k (0 < k < e) divides Π_{k=1}^{e−1}(1 − ζ^k) = e (IsPrimitiveRoot.prod_one_sub_pow_eq_order), a unit since p ∤ e; so the determinant is a unit.

**Acceptance.**

- K = ℚ_p, L = ℚ_p(ζ_p) = ℚ_p((−p)^{1/(p−1)}), e = p − 1.
- The hypothesis p ∤ e is used exactly once, to make e a unit; the statement is false in the wild case (Speiser).

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Matrix.det_vandermonde`
- `mathlib:IsPrimitiveRoot.prod_one_sub_pow_eq_order`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`

  > Let K be a p-adic field and let L/K be a totally and tamely ramified Galois extension of degree e. Then L/K has a normal integral basis. More precisely: ... for any choice of u_i ∈ O_K^×, α := Σ_{j=0}^{e−1} u_j π_L^j generates a NIB for L/K.

  Verbatim the node.

  > Assume further that L/K is Galois. Then K contains the eth roots of unity and is a cyclic Kummer extension with Kummer generator e√π.

  The Kummer structure used in the first step; it is the tame theorem of LocalFieldsRamification Layer 3 and is imported, not re-planned.

#### `local-tame-uniformiser` — Normalising a uniformiser of a tame local extension

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Local`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite tamely ramified Galois extension of nonarchimedean local fields with ramification index e, residue degree f and residue field of K of cardinality q. Then e divides q^f − 1, and there are a uniformiser π_L of L and a (q^f − 1)-st root of unity v in L with π_L^e = v·π_K.

**Hypotheses.**

- A is a complete discrete valuation ring with finite residue field k of cardinality q and characteristic p (the ring of integers of a nonarchimedean local field K, in the vocabulary of the LocalFieldsRamification roadmap); K = Frac A.
- L/K is a finite Galois extension with group G and B is the integral closure of A in L (the valuation ring of L); IsGaloisGroup G A B.
- L/K tamely ramified: p ∤ e.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Let L_0 be the maximal unramified subextension; L/L_0 is totally and tamely ramified Galois of degree e with group the inertia group I (LocalFieldsRamification Layers 2 and 3).
2. By the tame Galois criterion (LocalFieldsRamification Layer 3), I embeds in μ_e(L_0), and μ_e(L_0) injects into the residue field of L_0, which has q^f elements; so e | q^f − 1.
3. Write π_L^e = u·π_K with u ∈ 𝒪_L^×; let v be the Teichmüller lift of the residue of u (a (q^f − 1)-st root of unity, TauCeti.teichmuller).
4. v·π_K/π_L^e has residue 1; since p ∤ e, Hensel's lemma gives β ∈ 𝒪_L^× with β^e = v·π_K/π_L^e.
5. Replace π_L by βπ_L.

**Acceptance.**

- For K = ℚ_7 and L = ℚ_7(7^{1/3}): e = 3, f = 1, q = 7, and 3 | 6.
- In the wild case p | e the divisibility e | q^f − 1 fails, since q^f − 1 is prime to p.

**Prerequisites.**

- `tauceti:TauCeti.teichmuller`
- `tauceti:TauCeti.residue_teichmuller`
- `mathlib:HenselianLocalRing`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tamely-ramified`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-2-unramified-extensions-and-frobenius` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-3-ramification-the-tame-and-wild-cases-and-the-filtration` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Theorem 9.5, first paragraph after the diagram (printed p. 17).

  > By a minor adaptation of the proof of Proposition 9.2(i), there exists a prime element π_L of L and a (q^f − 1)-st root of unity v such that (8) π_L^e = vπ_K.

  The node, with the adaptation written out from the proof of Proposition 9.2(i).

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Theorem 9.5 (printed p. 17).

  > as L/K is tame, the ramification group of L/K embeds into L^× (see [Fis10, Theorem 6.11]); hence e divides q^f − 1.

  The divisibility half.

#### `local-tame-split-extension` — The split tame extension above a tame local extension

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Local`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite tamely ramified Galois extension of nonarchimedean local fields with ramification index e and residue degree f, and let L′ be the unramified extension of L of degree e. Then L′/K is Galois and tame; its maximal unramified subextension L′_0 has degree ef over K; there is π′ ∈ L′ with π′^e = π_K; K′ = K(π′) is totally and tamely ramified of degree e over K; L′_0 ∩ K′ = K and L′ = L′_0·K′. Writing I = Gal(L′/L′_0) and Γ = Gal(L′/K′), I is normal in G′ = Gal(L′/K), I ∩ Γ = 1 and IΓ = G′.

**Hypotheses.**

- A is a complete discrete valuation ring with finite residue field k of cardinality q and characteristic p (the ring of integers of a nonarchimedean local field K, in the vocabulary of the LocalFieldsRamification roadmap); K = Frac A.
- L/K is a finite Galois extension with group G and B is the integral closure of A in L (the valuation ring of L); IsGaloisGroup G A B.
- L/K tamely ramified with invariants e, f.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. L′/K is Galois as the compositum of L with the unramified (hence Galois) extension L′_0 of K of degree ef (LocalFieldsRamification Layer 2).
2. e(L′/K) = e and f(L′/K) = ef by multiplicativity in towers; so L′/K is tame (tamely-ramified, tower stability).
3. With π_L, v from local-tame-uniformiser: (q^{ef} − 1) = (q^f − 1)(q^{f(e−1)} + … + 1) and the second factor is ≡ e ≡ 0 mod e, so e(q^f − 1) | q^{ef} − 1 and v = u^e for a (q^{ef} − 1)-st root of unity u ∈ L′_0.
4. π′ = u⁻¹π_L satisfies π′^e = π_K; X^e − π_K is Eisenstein, so K′ = K(π′) is totally ramified of degree e (LocalFieldsRamification Layer 3).
5. An unramified and a totally ramified extension meet in K, and degrees give L′ = L′_0 K′; Galois theory gives the group-theoretic splitting.

**Acceptance.**

- For K = ℚ_7, L = ℚ_7(7^{1/3}) (e = 3, f = 1): L′ = L·ℚ_{7^3}, L′_0 = ℚ_{7^3}, K′ = L.
- If L/K is already totally ramified with π_L^e = π_K one may take L′ = L·(unramified of degree e) and K′ = L.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-tame-uniformiser`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tamely-ramified`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-2-unramified-extensions-and-frobenius` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-3-ramification-the-tame-and-wild-cases-and-the-filtration` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Theorem 9.5 (printed pp. 16–17).

  > Let e be the ramification degree of L/K and let L′/L be the unique unramified extension of degree e ... Letting K′ = K(π_{L′}) we see that L′/K is the compositum of the maximal unramified subextension L′_0/L and the totally ramified extension K′/K.

  The node collects the construction of the proof.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Remark 9.6 (printed p. 17).

  > The extension L′/K in the proof of Theorem 9.5 is referred to as ‘split’ because the group extension (9) 1 −→ I −→ G −→ G/I −→ 1 is split

  The group-theoretic splitting I ∩ Γ = 1, IΓ = G′.

#### `normal-integral-basis-of-split-extension` — Normal integral bases multiply across a split extension

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Local`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let N/K be a finite Galois extension with group G (of number fields or of nonarchimedean local fields), I a normal subgroup and Γ a subgroup with I ∩ Γ = 1 and IΓ = G; put N_0 = N^I and M = N^Γ. If β ∈ 𝒪_{N_0} generates a normal integral basis of N_0/K (group G/I ≅ Γ) and α ∈ 𝒪_M generates a normal integral basis of N/N_0 (group I), then αβ generates a normal integral basis of N/K.

**Hypotheses.**

- G = IΓ with I normal and I ∩ Γ = 1, so every g ∈ G is uniquely hγ with h ∈ I, γ ∈ Γ.
- α ∈ N^Γ and β ∈ N^I.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. 𝒪_N = ⊕_{h∈I} h(α)·𝒪_{N_0} and 𝒪_{N_0} = ⊕_{γ∈Γ} γ(β)·𝒪_K; combine the two bases (Module.Basis.smulTower).
2. For g = hγ: g(αβ) = h(γ(α)·γ(β)) = h(α)·h(γ(β)) = h(α)·γ(β), since γ fixes α and γ(β) ∈ N_0 is fixed by I (I normal).
3. So the products h(α)γ(β) are exactly the conjugates g(αβ), and they form an 𝒪_K-basis of 𝒪_N.

**Acceptance.**

- With I = 1: the statement is that β generates.
- In the proof of the local Noether theorem it is applied to L′/K with I the inertia group and Γ = Gal(L′/K′).

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Module.Basis.smulTower`
- `mathlib:Module.Basis.smulTower_apply`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Theorem 9.5, last display (printed p. 17).

  > Therefore O_{L′} = ⊕ τ^i(α)O_{L′_0} = ⊕ τ^i(α)σ^j(β)O_K. Since β is in L′_0, the fixed field of I = ⟨τ⟩, we have τ(β) = β. Since α is in K′, the fixed field of Γ = ⟨σ⟩, we have σ(α) = α.

  The node abstracts this step from the cyclic generators τ, σ to a split group G = IΓ.

#### `local-noether` — Noether's theorem for local fields

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Local`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of nonarchimedean local fields with group G. Then 𝒪_L has a normal integral basis over 𝒪_K if and only if L/K is tamely ramified.

**Hypotheses.**

- A is a complete discrete valuation ring with finite residue field k of cardinality q and characteristic p (the ring of integers of a nonarchimedean local field K, in the vocabulary of the LocalFieldsRamification roadmap); K = Frac A.
- L/K is a finite Galois extension with group G and B is the integral closure of A in L (the valuation ring of L); IsGaloisGroup G A B.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. If: build L′ ⊇ L as in local-tame-split-extension.
2. L′_0/K is unramified, so has a normal integral basis generator β (local-unramified-normal-integral-basis).
3. L′/L′_0 is totally and tamely ramified Galois of degree e with π′^e = π_K, π′ ∈ K′; α = Σ_{i<e} π′^i ∈ K′ generates a normal integral basis of L′/L′_0 (local-totally-tamely-ramified-normal-integral-basis with u_j = 1).
4. αβ generates a normal integral basis of L′/K (normal-integral-basis-of-split-extension).
5. L ⊆ L′ with L/K Galois, so Tr_{L′/L}(αβ) generates a normal integral basis of L/K (trace-of-normal-integral-basis-generator, whose proof is the same for local fields).
6. Only if: Speiser (tame-of-normal-integral-basis).

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ_2(i)/ℚ_2 has no normal integral basis; ℚ_p(ζ_p)/ℚ_p (p odd) has one.
- The global analogue fails: tameness does not give a normal integral basis of a number field in general (Noether's theorem gives only local freeness).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-tame-split-extension`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-unramified-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-totally-tamely-ramified-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis-of-split-extension`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/trace-of-normal-integral-basis-generator`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-of-normal-integral-basis`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Theorem 9.5 (printed p. 16).

  > Let L/K be a finite tame Galois extension of p-adic fields. Then L/K has a normal integral basis.

  The tame direction.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Corollary 9.10 (printed p. 19).

  > Let K be a p-adic field and let L/K be a finite Galois extension with group G. Then L/K has a normal integral basis if and only if L/K is tamely ramified.

  Verbatim the node.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Introduction (printed p. 153).

  > E. Noether [7] proved that if K/F is tamely ramified, then O_K has a normal basis everywhere locally.

  The attribution to Noether.

#### `locally-free-lattice` — Locally free lattices over an order

*definition.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.LocallyFree`.

**Statement.** Let A be a Dedekind domain with fraction field K and Λ an A-algebra that is finitely generated as an A-module (an A-order in the K-algebra K ⊗ Λ). A left Λ-module M is a locally free Λ-lattice of rank n if M is finitely generated over A and, for every nonzero prime v of A, the completion Â_v ⊗_A M is isomorphic to (Â_v ⊗_A Λ)^n as an Â_v ⊗_A Λ-module: there is an Â_v-linear isomorphism Â_v ⊗ M ≅ Â_v ⊗ Λ^n commuting with the action of every element of Λ. For Λ = A[G] and M = B with its group-ring structure, rank one local freeness says that at every v the completed module has a normal basis: Â_v ⊗ B = Â_v[G]·α_v.

**Hypotheses.**

- A Dedekind domain, K its fraction field, Â_v = v.adicCompletionIntegers K.
- Λ an A-algebra (possibly noncommutative), M a left Λ-module with the scalar tower A → Λ → M.
- The rank n is a natural number; rank zero forces M = 0.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define IsLocallyFreeOfRank as finite generation over A together with, for each HeightOneSpectrum v, an Â_v-linear equivalence commuting with the base changes of the maps DistribSMul.toLinearMap A M λ.
2. Free lattices: Λ^n is locally free of rank n (the identity equivalence).
3. Direct sums add ranks; isomorphic modules are simultaneously locally free.
4. Rank: tensoring the local isomorphism with Frac(Â_v) and comparing dimensions over K gives dim_K(K ⊗ M) = n·dim_K(K ⊗ Λ).
5. Group ring form: an Â_v[G]-isomorphism Â_v[G] ≅ Â_v ⊗ B is determined by the image b_1 of 1, and b_g = g·b_1.

**Acceptance.**

- A nonprincipal ideal I of a Dedekind domain A is locally free of rank one over Λ = A, but not free: local freeness does not imply freeness.
- ℤ[i] is not locally free of rank one over ℤ[C_2]: at 2 the completed module has no normal basis (local-noether).

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `LocallyFree.IsLocallyFreeOfRank` | data | M is a locally free Λ-lattice of rank n. |
| `LocallyFree.IsLocallyFreeOfRank.of_free` | constructor | Λ^n is locally free of rank n. |
| `LocallyFree.IsLocallyFreeOfRank.prod` | structure | M ⊕ N is locally free of rank n + m. |
| `LocallyFree.IsLocallyFreeOfRank.of_linearEquiv` | compatibility | Local freeness is invariant under Λ-isomorphism. |
| `LocallyFree.IsLocallyFreeOfRank.finrank` | projection | dim_K(K ⊗ M) = n·dim_K(K ⊗ Λ). |
| `LocallyFree.isLocallyFreeOfRank_one_groupRing_iff` | characterisation | For Λ = A[G] and M = B: rank one local freeness is the existence at every v of a basis of Â_v ⊗ B indexed by G with b_g = g·b_1. |
| `LocallyFree.not_free_of_not_isPrincipal` | example | A nonzero nonprincipal ideal of A is locally free of rank one over A and not free. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/noether-theorem`: the conclusion of Noether's theorem
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-class-group`: the locally free class group is generated by the classes of these lattices
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice-is-projective`: locally free lattices define classes in K₀
- Taylor 1981, Introduction: By Noether's theorem 𝓞_M is a locally free ℤΓ-module, and Cl(ℤΓ) is the class group of locally free ℤΓ-modules

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `LocallyFree.test_self` (degenerate): Λ is locally free of rank one over itself.
- `LocallyFree.test_rank_zero` (degenerate): A locally free lattice of rank zero is zero.
- `LocallyFree.test_ideal_not_free` (non-example): A nonzero nonprincipal ideal of a Dedekind domain is locally free of rank one but not free.
- `LocallyFree.test_gaussian_not_locallyFree` (non-example): ℤ[i] is not locally free of rank one over ℤ[Gal(ℚ(i)/ℚ)].

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum.adicCompletionIntegers`
- `mathlib:LinearMap.baseChange`
- `mathlib:Module.Finite`
- `mathlib:IsDedekindDomain`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 10.2 (printed p. 20).

  > Two Λ-lattices M and N are in the same genus, or are locally isomorphic, if M_p ≅ N_p as Λ_p-modules for each p; in this case we write M ∨ N. We say that M is locally free over rank n if M ∨ Λ^(n).

  Verbatim the node, with M_p the completion (Section 4.2 of the source defines M_p = O_p ⊗_O M).

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Example 10.6 (printed p. 20).

  > Then O_L is a locally free O_K[G]-module of rank 1 if and only if O_{L,p} ≅ O_{K_p}[G] (as O_{K_p}[G]-modules) for each prime ideal p of O_K.

  The group-ring characterisation in the API.

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, 20.2.8 (printed p. 318).

  > A left O-lattice M is locally free of rank r ≥ 1 if M_𝔭 ≃ O_𝔭^⊕r as left O-modules for all primes 𝔭 ⊆ R.

  The same definition for orders.

#### `locally-free-lattice-is-projective` — Locally free lattices are projective

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.LocallyFree`.

**Statement.** Let A be a Dedekind domain and Λ an A-algebra finitely generated as an A-module. A locally free Λ-lattice of rank n is a finitely generated projective Λ-module.

**Hypotheses.**

- Λ is module-finite over the noetherian ring A, hence left noetherian; M is finitely presented over Λ.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Projectivity of a finitely presented module is the exactness of Hom_Λ(M, −) on surjections.
2. For a flat commutative A-algebra A′, A′ ⊗ Hom_Λ(M, N) ≅ Hom_{A′⊗Λ}(A′ ⊗ M, A′ ⊗ N) since M is finitely presented.
3. Exactness of a sequence of A-modules is checked after localisation at every maximal ideal (Mathlib's local properties), and A_(v) → Â_v is faithfully flat (flat local homomorphism of noetherian local rings).
4. At every v, Â_v ⊗ M ≅ (Â_v ⊗ Λ)^n is free, so Hom out of it is exact; descend.

**Acceptance.**

- Λ^n is projective.
- A nonzero ideal of a Dedekind domain is projective (rank one, Λ = A).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice`
- `mathlib:Module.Projective`
- `mathlib:Module.FaithfullyFlat`
- `mathlib:Module.FaithfullyFlat.of_flat_of_isLocalHom`
- `mathlib:AdicCompletion.flat_of_isNoetherian`
- `mathlib:Module.FinitePresentation`
- `mathlib:Module.projective_of_localization_maximal`

**Sources.**

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, 20.2.6 and 20.2.8 (printed p. 318).

  > we see that P is projective as a left O-module if and only if P_(𝔭) is projective as a left O_(𝔭)-module for all primes 𝔭 ⊆ R ... If follows from 20.2.5 and 20.2.6 that a locally free O-lattice is projective.

  Verbatim the node; the passage from completion to localisation is faithful flatness.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 14, first paragraph (printed p. 26).

  > Let X be a (left) locally free A-lattice of rank d. (We shall henceforth drop the qualifier ‘left’.) Then X is a projective A-module (proof omitted).

  The same statement, whose proof the source omits.

#### `sublattice-eq-of-completions` — Lattices are determined by their completions

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.LocallyFree`.

**Statement.** Let A be a Dedekind domain, V an A-module, and N ⊆ P two A-submodules of V with P finitely generated. If Â_v ⊗ N → Â_v ⊗ P is surjective for every nonzero prime v of A, then N = P.

**Hypotheses.**

- A Dedekind domain; P finitely generated over A.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. P/N is a finitely generated A-module with Â_v ⊗ (P/N) = 0 for all v (right exactness of the tensor product).
2. A_(v) → Â_v is faithfully flat, so (P/N)_(v) = 0 for every maximal ideal v.
3. A module whose localisations at all maximal ideals vanish is zero (Module.eq_zero_of_localization_maximal); hence N = P.

**Acceptance.**

- For A = ℤ, V = ℚ, N = 2ℤ ⊆ P = ℤ: the map fails to be onto exactly at v = 2.
- Used to identify the associated order of a tame extension with the group ring.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Submodule.eq_of_localization_maximal`
- `mathlib:Module.FaithfullyFlat.of_flat_of_isLocalHom`
- `mathlib:AdicCompletion.flat_of_isNoetherian`
- `mathlib:LinearMap.baseChange`

**Sources.**

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, Corollary 9.4.7 and Lemma 9.5.3 (printed pp. 140–142).

  > In particular, Lemma 9.5.3 implies that in the local-global dictionary for lattices over a Dedekind domain R (Theorem 9.4.9), we may also work with collections of R_𝔭-lattices (N_𝔭)_𝔭 over the completions at primes.

  The node is the containment half of the local–global dictionary, stated with completions.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 4.2 and Lemma 4.1 (printed p. 6).

  > For any maximal ideal p of O, write O_p for the the completion lim O/p^n of O at p. ... For any O-module M, we write M_p for O_p ⊗_O M

  The convention for completed lattices used throughout the layer.

#### `completed-ring-of-integers-is-induced` — The completed ring of integers is induced from a decomposition group

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of number fields with group G, v a nonzero prime of 𝓞_K and w a prime of 𝓞_L above v with decomposition group G_w, identified with Gal(L_w/K_v). Then, as 𝒪̂_v[G]-modules, 𝒪̂_v ⊗ 𝓞_L ≅ Π_{w′|v} 𝒪_{L_{w′}} ≅ 𝒪̂_v[G] ⊗_{𝒪̂_v[G_w]} 𝒪_{L_w} (induction along G_w ⊆ G, with left modules).

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois; v, w as stated; the completions are the canonical ones of the NumberFieldArithmetic roadmap.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. NumberFieldArithmetic Layer 5.7 gives the integral semilocal equivalence 𝒪̂_v ⊗ 𝓞_L ≅ Π_{w′|v} 𝒪_{L_{w′}} with its pure-tensor formula; it is G-equivariant, G permuting the factors.
