2. G acts transitively on the primes over v with stabiliser G_w; NumberFieldArithmetic Layer 5.6 identifies G_w with Gal(L_w/K_v) through decompositionHom.
3. Choose left coset representatives R of G_w in G; the product is ⊕_{g∈R} g·𝒪_{L_w}, which is the induced module 𝒪̂_v[G] ⊗_{𝒪̂_v[G_w]} 𝒪_{L_w}.

**Acceptance.**

- For v unramified and split completely, G_w = 1 and the module is 𝒪̂_v[G] ⊗ 𝒪̂_v = 𝒪̂_v[G].
- For v inert, G_w = G and the statement is 𝒪̂_v ⊗ 𝓞_L = 𝒪_{L_w}.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module`
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`
- `mathlib:Ideal.isPretransitive_of_isGaloisGroup`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-5-the-global-local-dictionary-at-finite-places` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Theorem 10.7 (printed p. 20).

  > Let G_P be the decomposition group of P ... Let R be a set of representatives of the left cosets of G_P in G. Then O_{K_p}[G] = ⊕_{g∈R} g(O_{K_p}[G_P]). We therefore have ⊕_{P|p} O_{L_P} ≅ O_{L_P} ⊗_{O_{K_p}[G_P]} O_{K_p}[G]

  The node, with the induction written for left modules (see the recorded misprint).

#### `locally-free-of-tame` — Noether: tame extensions have locally free rings of integers

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite tamely ramified Galois extension of number fields with group G. Then 𝓞_L is a locally free 𝓞_K[G]-lattice of rank one.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois and tame.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Fix v and w | v. L_w/K_v is Galois with group G_w (NumberFieldArithmetic Layer 5.6) and tamely ramified, since its ramification index is e(w|v) (NumberFieldArithmetic Layer 5.5).
2. By the local Noether theorem 𝒪_{L_w} = 𝒪̂_v[G_w]·α_w.
3. By completed-ring-of-integers-is-induced, 𝒪̂_v ⊗ 𝓞_L ≅ 𝒪̂_v[G] ⊗_{𝒪̂_v[G_w]} 𝒪̂_v[G_w] ≅ 𝒪̂_v[G].

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ: locally free (indeed free).
- ℚ(i)/ℚ: not locally free at 2 (wild).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-noether`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/completed-ring-of-integers-is-induced`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tamely-ramified`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-5-the-global-local-dictionary-at-finite-places` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Theorem 10.7 (printed p. 20).

  > Let L/K be a finite tame Galois extension of number fields with group G. Then O_L is a locally free O_K[G]-module of rank 1.

  Verbatim the node.

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Introduction and Results (printed p. 41).

  > Let M/N be a tame Galois extension of number fields with Γ = Gal(M/N). By Noether's theorem (cf. [N]) we know that the ring of integers of M, 𝔒_M, is a locally free ℤΓ module.

  The theorem as Taylor uses it (over ℤΓ; see ring-of-integers-locally-free-over-integral-group-ring).

#### `noether-theorem` — Noether's theorem

*theorem.* **Planet:** *Noether's theorem*. Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of number fields with group G. The following are equivalent: (i) L/K is tamely ramified; (ii) 𝓞_L is a locally free 𝓞_K[G]-lattice of rank one; (iii) 𝓞_L is a projective 𝓞_K[G]-module.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. (i) ⇒ (ii): locally-free-of-tame.
2. (ii) ⇒ (iii): locally-free-lattice-is-projective.
3. (iii) ⇒ (i): trace-surjective-of-projective and tame-iff-trace-surjective.
4. (i) ⇒ (iii) also holds directly: projective-of-tame.

**Acceptance.**

- For ℚ(i)/ℚ all three fail.
- Local freeness does not give freeness: see locally-free-not-free-example and the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-of-tame`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice-is-projective`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/trace-surjective-of-projective`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-iff-trace-surjective`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 1.3 (printed p. 2).

  > In fact, it is always the case that if L/K has a normal integral basis, then L/K must be (at most) tamely ramified. Though the converse is not true in general for number fields, it does hold when L/K is an extension of p-adic fields (Noether’s Theorem).

  The local form, combined with Theorem 10.7 and Example 10.6, is the equivalence (i) ⇔ (ii).

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Proposition 1.3 (printed p. 156).

  > K/F tamely ramified implies any ambiguous ideal 𝔄 of K is O_F H-projective.

  The projectivity form (i) ⇒ (iii).

#### `ring-of-integers-locally-free-over-integral-group-ring` — The ring of integers is locally free over ℤ[G]

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite tamely ramified Galois extension of number fields with group G. Then 𝓞_L, with the action of ℤ[G] through G, is a locally free ℤ[G]-lattice of rank [K : ℚ].

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois and tame.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. At a rational prime p: ℤ_p ⊗ 𝓞_L = (ℤ_p ⊗ 𝓞_K) ⊗_{𝓞_K} 𝓞_L = Π_{v|p} 𝒪̂_v ⊗_{𝓞_K} 𝓞_L (NumberFieldArithmetic Layer 5.7 over ℚ).
2. Each factor is 𝒪̂_v[G] (locally-free-of-tame), so the product is (ℤ_p ⊗ 𝓞_K)[G].
3. ℤ_p ⊗ 𝓞_K is free of rank [K : ℚ] over ℤ_p, so the module is ℤ_p[G]^{[K:ℚ]}.

**Acceptance.**

- For K = ℚ the rank is one and the statement is locally-free-of-tame.
- The rank is [K : ℚ], not 1: 𝓞_K[G] itself is free of rank [K : ℚ] over ℤ[G].

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-of-tame`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-5-the-global-local-dictionary-at-finite-places` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Introduction and Results (printed p. 41).

  > By Noether's theorem (cf. [N]) we know that the ring of integers of M, 𝔒_M, is a locally free ℤΓ module.

  The node, with the rank made explicit.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Remark 3.17 (printed p. 6).

  > If O_L is free over O_K[G] (necessarily of rank 1), then O_L is also free over Z[G] (a Z-order in Q[G]) of rank [K : Q] (this is because O_K is free over Z of rank [K : Q], so O_K[G] is free over Z[G] of rank [K : Q]).

  The rank computation.

#### `locally-free-not-free-example` — Local freeness is separate from freeness

*application.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/LocallyFree`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.LocallyFree`.

**Statement.** Let A be a Dedekind domain with a nonzero nonprincipal ideal I (for example A = ℤ[√−5], I = (2, 1 + √−5)). Then I is a locally free A-lattice of rank one (over the trivial group ring A[1] = A) but is not free. Hence a locally free lattice over an order need not be free, and the class of a locally free lattice in the locally free class group can be nonzero.

**Hypotheses.**

- A Dedekind domain; I ≠ 0 nonprincipal.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Locally free: every completion Â_v is a discrete valuation ring, so Â_v ⊗ I is a nonzero ideal of a DVR (flatness), hence free of rank one.
2. Not free: a free ideal of rank one is principal; I is finitely generated of rank one, so free would mean I ≅ A, i.e. I principal.

**Acceptance.**

- The acceptance gate of the layer: local freeness is separate from global freeness.
- Its class in Cl(A) = Pic(A) is the ideal class of I, nonzero (locally-free-class-group, classGroup_test_ideal).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice`
- `mathlib:IsDedekindDomain`
- `mathlib:ClassGroup`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Examples 3.3(i) (printed p. 4).

  > A nonzero ideal I of O_K is an O_K-lattice in K, and is free if and only if I is principal.

  The non-freeness half.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 15.1 (printed p. 28).

  > It is important to note that if [O_L] is the trivial element of Cl(O_K[G]), this does not necessarily imply that O_L is free over O_K[G].

  The source's own separation of local and global freeness.

---

### CA.7.C The Hilbert–Speiser theorem, resolvents and discriminants

Over ℚ tameness does give a normal integral basis for abelian fields: ζ_p generates for ℚ(ζ_p), arithmetically disjoint composita multiply generators, so ζ_n generates for n odd squarefree, and the Gauss period generates for every subfield; conversely a tame abelian field has odd squarefree level (Kronecker–Weber and the ramification of cyclotomic fields). Resolvents detect normal basis generators, and the discriminant of a normal basis is the product of squared resolvents (Dedekind's group determinant), which gives a discriminant criterion for a normal integral basis over ℚ.

#### `normal-integral-basis-of-arithmetically-disjoint-compositum` — Normal integral bases of arithmetically disjoint composita

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/HilbertSpeiser`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let K₁, K₂ ⊆ N be Galois number fields over ℚ, linearly disjoint (their discriminants coprime, which forces this), with K₁K₂ = N, so that Gal(N/ℚ) = Gal(K₁/ℚ) × Gal(K₂/ℚ). If α_i generates a normal integral basis of K_i/ℚ, then α₁α₂ generates a normal integral basis of N/ℚ. More generally (Johnston 6.4(i)), over a base F with coprime different ideals, a normal integral basis of L/F stays one of LK/K.

**Hypotheses.**

- K₁, K₂ Galois over ℚ with coprime discriminants and compositum N.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Coprime discriminants give linear disjointness for Galois fields (NumberField.linearDisjoint_of_isGalois_isCoprime_discr) and coprime extended differents (NumberField.isCoprime_differentIdeal_of_isCoprime_discr).
2. Module.Basis.ofIsCoprimeDifferentIdeal lifts the ℤ-basis (σα₂)_σ of 𝓞_{K₂} to a 𝓞_{K₁}-basis of 𝓞_N.
3. Combine with the ℤ-basis (τα₁)_τ of 𝓞_{K₁} (Module.Basis.smulTower): (τα₁·σα₂)_{τ,σ} is a ℤ-basis of 𝓞_N.
4. Under Gal(N/ℚ) = Gal(K₁/ℚ) × Gal(K₂/ℚ), (τ, σ)(α₁α₂) = τα₁·σα₂.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(ζ_3) and ℚ(ζ_5): ζ_3ζ_5 = ζ_15 generates for ℚ(ζ_15).
- The coprimality hypothesis is needed: ℚ(i) and ℚ(√2) are not arithmetically disjoint.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Module.Basis.ofIsCoprimeDifferentIdeal`
- `mathlib:Module.Basis.ofIsCoprimeDifferentIdeal_apply`
- `mathlib:NumberField.linearDisjoint_of_isGalois_isCoprime_discr`
- `mathlib:NumberField.isCoprime_differentIdeal_of_isCoprime_discr`
- `mathlib:Module.Basis.smulTower`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proposition 6.4 (printed p. 11).

  > Let F be a number field or a p-adic field, and let L and K be arithmetically disjoint extensions of F. Suppose that L/F is Galois with group G. (i) Suppose α ∈ O_L such that O_L = O_F[G] · α. Then O_{LK} = O_K[G] · α.

  Part (i); part (ii), with K/F Galois of group H and generator β, gives O_{LK} = O_F[G × H]·αβ, which is the node over ℚ.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Proposition 1.8 (printed p. 157).

  > Let F be an algebraic number field with K_1, K_2 Galois extensions of F such that K_1 ∩ K_2 = F and the discriminants D_i = D(K_i/F), i = 1,2, are relatively prime.

  The same statement, proved through discriminants.

#### `prime-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis` — ζ_p generates a normal integral basis of ℚ(ζ_p)

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/HilbertSpeiser`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** For an odd prime p, the primitive p-th root of unity ζ_p generates a normal integral basis of ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ: its conjugates ζ_p, ζ_p², …, ζ_p^{p−1} form a ℤ-basis of ℤ[ζ_p].

**Hypotheses.**

- p an odd prime.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. ℤ[ζ_p] = 𝓞 with power basis 1, ζ_p, …, ζ_p^{p−2} (IsPrimitiveRoot.integralPowerBasis, dimension φ(p) = p − 1).
2. ζ_p is a unit, so multiplying by ζ_p gives the basis ζ_p, …, ζ_p^{p−1}.
3. These are exactly the conjugates of ζ_p (Gal(ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ) ≅ (ℤ/p)^×).

**Acceptance.**

- p = 3: ζ_3, ζ_3² = −1 − ζ_3 form a ℤ-basis of ℤ[ζ_3].
- For p = 2 the field is ℚ and ζ_2 = −1 is a unit, which generates trivially.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:IsPrimitiveRoot.integralPowerBasis`
- `mathlib:IsCyclotomicExtension.Rat.galEquivZMod`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Proposition 6.6, first paragraph (printed p. 12).

  > Then the ring of integers of Q(ζ) is Z[ζ], which has Z-basis 1, ζ, ζ², ..., ζ^{p−2}. Since ζ is a unit, ζZ[ζ] = Z[ζ] and so ζ, ζ², ..., ζ^{p−1} is also a Z-basis of Z[ζ]. However, these are precisely the Galois conjugates of ζ

  Verbatim the node.

- conrad-linear-characters-2026 — Keith Conrad, *Linear independence of characters*, Example 3.2 (p. 2).

  > but {ζ_p, ζ_p², . . . , ζ_p^{p−1}} is a normal basis: its elements are Q-conjugates and it is a basis since it is the ζ_p-multiples of the basis B.

  The rational statement.

#### `squarefree-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis` — ζ_n generates a normal integral basis for odd squarefree n

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/HilbertSpeiser`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let n be odd and squarefree. Then ζ_n generates a normal integral basis of ℚ(ζ_n)/ℚ.

**Hypotheses.**

- n odd squarefree.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Induct on the number of prime factors; n = 1 is trivial and n = p is prime-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis.
2. Write n = pm with p ∤ m. ℚ(ζ_p) and ℚ(ζ_m) have coprime discriminants (IsCyclotomicExtension.Rat.natAbs_discr) and compositum ℚ(ζ_n) (IntermediateField.isCyclotomicExtension_lcm_sup).
3. By the compositum lemma ζ_pζ_m generates; it is a primitive n-th root of unity, and all primitive n-th roots are conjugate, so ζ_n generates.

**Acceptance.**

- n = 15: ζ_15 generates.
- n = 9 (not squarefree): ζ_9 does not generate; its conjugates sum to 0.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/prime-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis-of-arithmetically-disjoint-compositum`
- `mathlib:IsCyclotomicExtension.Rat.natAbs_discr`
- `mathlib:IntermediateField.isCyclotomicExtension_lcm_sup`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Proposition 6.6, second paragraph (printed p. 12).

  > Let n ∈ N be odd and square-free. We shall show that Q(ζ_n)/Q has NIB generated by ζ_n. Write n = pm where p is and odd prime; then m is odd, square-free and coprime to p.

  Verbatim the node.

#### `gauss-period-normal-integral-basis` — Gauss periods generate normal integral bases

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/HilbertSpeiser`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let n be odd and squarefree and K ⊆ ℚ(ζ_n) a subfield. Then K/ℚ is Galois and the Gauss period Tr_{ℚ(ζ_n)/K}(ζ_n) generates a normal integral basis of K/ℚ.

**Hypotheses.**

- n odd squarefree; K ⊆ ℚ(ζ_n).

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. ℚ(ζ_n)/ℚ is abelian, so every subfield is Galois over ℚ.
2. Apply trace-of-normal-integral-basis-generator to the generator ζ_n of squarefree-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis.

**Acceptance.**

- The quadratic subfield ℚ(√−3) of ℚ(ζ_3) is ℚ(ζ_3) itself; for ℚ(√5) ⊆ ℚ(ζ_5), Tr(ζ_5) = ζ_5 + ζ_5⁴ = (−1 + √5)/2 generates.
- The period generates for every subfield, not only for ℚ(ζ_n) itself.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/squarefree-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/trace-of-normal-integral-basis-generator`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proposition 6.6 (printed p. 11).

  > Let n ∈ N be odd and square-free and let K ⊆ Q(ζ_n). Then K/Q has a normal integral basis generated by Tr_{Q(ζ_n)/K}(ζ_n).

  Verbatim the node.

- conrad-linear-characters-2026 — Keith Conrad, *Linear independence of characters*, Example 3.9 (p. 6).

  > Gauss called these sums “periods” [6, art. 343], with ζ_p = e^{2πi/p}.

  The name.

#### `tame-abelian-field-in-squarefree-cyclotomic-field` — A tame abelian number field has odd squarefree cyclotomic level

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/HilbertSpeiser`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let K/ℚ be a finite abelian extension that is tamely ramified. Then K embeds in ℚ(ζ_n) for some odd squarefree n.

**Hypotheses.**

- K/ℚ finite abelian and tame.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. By Kronecker–Weber (ClassFieldTheory Layer 13) K ⊆ ℚ(ζ_n) for some n; take n least. Then n ≢ 2 mod 4 since ℚ(ζ_{2m}) = ℚ(ζ_m) for m odd.
2. Let p | n be odd, n = p^r m with p ∤ m. In ℚ(ζ_n) the inertia group of p is Gal(ℚ(ζ_n)/ℚ(ζ_m)) ≅ (ℤ/p^r)^× of order p^{r−1}(p − 1) (Mathlib: ramification index p^{r−1}(p − 1), and the inertia group has order the ramification index).
3. Its image in Gal(K/ℚ) is the inertia group of K at p, of order e_p(K), prime to p by tameness; so the p-Sylow subgroup Gal(ℚ(ζ_n)/ℚ(ζ_{pm})) fixes K and K ⊆ ℚ(ζ_{pm}); minimality forces r = 1.
4. If 2 | n then 4 | n and the inertia group at 2, Gal(ℚ(ζ_n)/ℚ(ζ_{n/2^r})), is a 2-group; tameness makes its image trivial, so K ⊆ ℚ(ζ_{n/2^r}), contradicting minimality. Hence n is odd.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(√5) has level 5; ℚ(√−1) has level 4 and is wild.
- ℚ(√2) has level 8 and is wild at 2.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tamely-ramified`
- `mathlib:IsCyclotomicExtension.Rat.ramificationIdx_eq`
- `mathlib:Ideal.card_inertia_eq_ramificationIdxIn`
- `mathlib:Ideal.ramificationIdxIn_mul_ramificationIdxIn`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-13-norm-theorems-and-class-fields` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proof of Theorem 8.4, (iii) implies (i) (printed p. 14).

  > Suppose that K/Q is tamely ramified. Let p be an odd prime dividing n and write n = mp^r with p not dividing m. ... If r > 1 then [Q(ζ_n) : Q(ζ_{pm})] = p^{r−1}, so K is contained in Q(ζ_{pm}) and p² ∤ n.

  The node; n is the conductor, the least level, as in Definition 8.2.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Theorem 8.1 (printed p. 14).

  > Every finite abelian extension of Q is contained in a cyclotomic field.

  Kronecker–Weber, imported from the ClassFieldTheory roadmap.

#### `hilbert-speiser` — The Hilbert–Speiser theorem

*theorem.* **Planet:** *Hilbert–Speiser theorem*. Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/HilbertSpeiser`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let K/ℚ be a finite abelian extension. The following are equivalent: (i) K has a normal integral basis over ℚ; (ii) K/ℚ is tamely ramified; (iii) K ⊆ ℚ(ζ_n) for some odd squarefree n. When they hold, the Gauss period Tr_{ℚ(ζ_n)/K}(ζ_n) generates a normal integral basis.

**Hypotheses.**

- K/ℚ finite abelian (IsAbelianGalois ℚ K).

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. (iii) ⇒ (i): gauss-period-normal-integral-basis.
2. (i) ⇒ (ii): Speiser (tame-of-normal-integral-basis).
3. (ii) ⇒ (iii): tame-abelian-field-in-squarefree-cyclotomic-field.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(√5), ℚ(√−3), ℚ(ζ_7) have normal integral bases; ℚ(i), ℚ(√2), ℚ(√−2) do not.
- The analogue over K ≠ ℚ fails: tameness gives only local freeness (Noether), and ℚ is the only Hilbert–Speiser field (Greither–Replogle–Rubin–Srivastav, quoted by Ferri–Greither).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/gauss-period-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-of-normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-abelian-field-in-squarefree-cyclotomic-field`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Theorem 8.4 (printed p. 14).

  > Let K be a finite abelian extension of Q of conductor n. Then K/Q has a normal integral basis if and only if K/Q is tamely ramified, or equivalently, n is odd and square-free. Furthermore, when this is the case, one can take α = Tr_{Q(ζn)/K}(ζn) to be a generator.

  The node; condition (iii) replaces "the conductor is odd squarefree" by the existence of an odd squarefree level, which is equivalent and needs no conductor.

- ferri-greither-tame-galois-2019 — Fabio Ferri and Cornelius Greither, *Tame Galois module structure revisited*, Introduction (p. 1).

  > The Hilbert-Speiser theorem states that for abelian number fields tameness is equivalent to the existence of a normal integral basis over Q, while in general we only have that an extension with NIB has to be tame.

  The theorem and its failure over larger bases.

#### `lagrange-resolvent` — Lagrange resolvents and the resolvend

*construction.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Resolvent`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.GaloisResolvent`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension with group G, E a field containing L, and χ : G → E^× a character. The resolvent of a ∈ L at χ is ⟨a | χ⟩ = Σ_{σ∈G} χ(σ⁻¹)·σ(a) ∈ E. The resolvend of a is r_G(a) = Σ_σ σ(a)·σ⁻¹ ∈ L[G], so that χ applied to r_G(a) is ⟨a | χ⟩. The resolvent is K-linear in a, satisfies ⟨τ(a) | χ⟩ = χ(τ)⟨a | χ⟩, equals the trace at the trivial character, and for abelian G (with E containing enough roots of unity) a is a normal basis generator exactly when all its resolvents are nonzero.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois; E an L-algebra field; characters valued in E^×.
- Convention (Fröhlich, Taylor): χ(σ⁻¹) in the resolvent, σ⁻¹ in the resolvend.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define both sums.
2. Equivariance: ⟨τa | χ⟩ = Σ_σ χ(σ⁻¹)στ(a) = Σ_ρ χ(τρ⁻¹)ρ(a) = χ(τ)⟨a | χ⟩; r_G(τa) = τ·r_G(a) likewise.
3. Trivial character: Σ_σ σ(a) = Tr_{L/K}(a) (trace_eq_sum_automorphisms).
4. Normal basis criterion: combine the resolvent–discriminant formula with nonvanishing of the discriminant exactly for bases.

**Acceptance.**

- For a ∈ K and χ nontrivial, ⟨a | χ⟩ = a·Σ_σ χ(σ⁻¹) = 0.
- For ℚ(i)/ℚ and a = 1 + i: ⟨a | 1⟩ = 2 and ⟨a | sgn⟩ = 2i.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `GaloisResolvent.resolvent` | data | ⟨a | χ⟩ = Σ_σ χ(σ⁻¹)σ(a). |
| `GaloisResolvent.resolvend` | data | r_G(a) = Σ_σ σ(a)σ⁻¹ ∈ L[G]. |
| `GaloisResolvent.resolvent_smul` | relation | ⟨τ(a) | χ⟩ = χ(τ)·⟨a | χ⟩. |
| `GaloisResolvent.resolvent_one` | compatibility | At the trivial character the resolvent is the trace. |
| `GaloisResolvent.resolvent_add` | simp | The resolvent is additive in a. |
| `GaloisResolvent.resolvend_smul` | relation | r_G(τa) = τ·r_G(a). |
| `GaloisResolvent.isGenerator_iff_forall_resolvent_ne_zero` | characterisation | For abelian G with |Hom(G, E^×)| = |G|, a is a normal basis generator exactly when every resolvent is nonzero. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/discriminant-of-normal-basis-resolvent-formula`: the discriminant of the conjugates is the product of the squared resolvents
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis-discriminant-criterion`: the integral criterion is read off the resolvents
- Taylor 1981, (2.2): the resolvend of a local normal integral basis generator represents the class of 𝓞_M

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `GaloisResolvent.resolvent_test_trivial` (compatibility): At the trivial character the resolvent is the trace.
- `GaloisResolvent.resolvent_test_base` (non-example): The resolvent of an element of K at a nontrivial character is 0.
- `GaloisResolvent.resolvent_test_quadratic` (computation): In a quadratic extension with σx = −x, the resolvent of 1 + x at the sign character is 2x.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:trace_eq_sum_automorphisms`
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/rational-normal-basis-map`

**Sources.**

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Section 2, (2.2) (printed p. 44).

  > we can find a ∈ 𝔒_M so that for all l ∈ S a · 𝔒_{N,l}Γ = 𝔒_{M,l}. ... Then we define A ∈ 𝔒_{M̂}Γ by (2.2) A = Π_σ (Σ_{γ∈Γ} a^{γσ} γ^{−1}).

  The resolvend Σ_γ a^γ γ^{−1}, the factor of Taylor's representative, is the node's r_G.

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Section 1 (printed p. 43).

  > Let χ be a character of Γ which is afforded by a representation T: Γ → GL_n(F). ... Then, for any x ∈ 𝔒_{L,l}Γ*, we write Det(x)(χ) = det(T(x)).

  Evaluating at a character: for abelian χ, Det(r_G(a))(χ) is the resolvent.

#### `group-determinant-factorisation` — Dedekind's factorisation of the group determinant

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Resolvent`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.GaloisResolvent`.

**Statement.** Let G be a finite abelian group and E a field such that G has |G| characters with values in E^× (E contains a primitive root of unity of order the exponent of G). For variables (X_g)_{g∈G}, det(X_{gh⁻¹})_{g,h} = Π_{χ} Σ_{g} χ(g) X_g.

**Hypotheses.**

- G finite abelian; E has |G| characters of G.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. For each χ the vector (χ(h))_h is an eigenvector of the group matrix (X_{gh⁻¹}) with eigenvalue Σ_k χ(k⁻¹)X_k, since Σ_h X_{gh⁻¹}χ(h) = χ(g)Σ_k χ(k⁻¹)X_k; as χ runs over the characters so does χ⁻¹.
2. The |G| character vectors are linearly independent (independence of characters), so the group matrix is diagonalisable with these eigenvalues, and its determinant is their product.

**Acceptance.**

- G = ℤ/2: det [[X_0, X_1],[X_1, X_0]] = (X_0 + X_1)(X_0 − X_1).
- G trivial: det(X_e) = X_e.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:linearIndependent_monoidHom`
- `mathlib:Matrix.det_vandermonde`

**Sources.**

- conrad-group-determinant-2010 — Keith Conrad, *The origin of representation theory*, Theorem 2 (p. 5).

  > Theorem 2. Let G be a finite abelian group. Then det(X_{gh^{−1}}) = Π_{χ∈Ĝ} (Σ_{g∈G} χ(g)X_g), where Ĝ is the character group of G, namely the group of homomorphisms from G to C^×.

  The node over ℂ; the first proof works over any field with a full set of characters, as the source remarks for characteristic p.

#### `discriminant-of-normal-basis-resolvent-formula` — The discriminant of a normal basis is the product of squared resolvents

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Resolvent`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.GaloisResolvent`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite abelian extension with group G, E ⊇ L a field with |G| characters of G, and a ∈ L. Then disc_{L/K}(σa)_{σ∈G} = det(Tr_{L/K}(σa·τa))_{σ,τ} = Π_χ ⟨a | χ⟩² in E.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite abelian; E as in the group determinant lemma.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. disc(σa) = det(ρσ(a))_{ρ,σ}² (Algebra.discr_eq_det_embeddingsMatrixReindex_pow_two, with the embeddings the automorphisms).
2. det(ρσ(a)) = ±det(ρσ⁻¹(a)) (reindex σ ↦ σ⁻¹), the group determinant at X_g = g(a).
3. Apply the factorisation; Σ_g χ(g)g(a) = ⟨a | χ̄⟩ and χ ↦ χ̄ permutes the characters.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(√5)/ℚ, a = (1 + √5)/2: ⟨a | 1⟩ = 1, ⟨a | sgn⟩ = √5, product of squares 5 = disc(a, a′).
- ℚ(i)/ℚ, a = 1 + i: 4·(2i)² = −16 = disc(1 + i, 1 − i).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/group-determinant-factorisation`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/lagrange-resolvent`
- `mathlib:Algebra.discr`
- `mathlib:Algebra.discr_eq_det_embeddingsMatrixReindex_pow_two`

**Sources.**

- conrad-group-determinant-2010 — Keith Conrad, *The origin of representation theory*, Section 3 (p. 4).

  > Dedekind had reasons to consider the discriminant of n elements formed by the Q-conjugates σ_i(α) of a single element α. ... Let x_σ = σ(α). Then this is the determinant of the matrix (a_{σ,τ}) doubly indexed by G, where a_{σ,τ} = x_{στ}.

  The link between the discriminant of the conjugates and the group determinant.

- ullom-normal-bases-1969 — S. Ullom, *Normal bases in Galois extensions of number fields*, Proof of Proposition 1.8 (printed p. 157).

  > The square of the determinant Δ[α_i] = det(σ_i^{(j)} α_i) is the discriminant (with respect to K_i/F) of the O_F G_i-module generated by α_i.

  The same identity used for normal integral bases.

#### `normal-integral-basis-discriminant-criterion` — The discriminant criterion for a normal integral basis over ℚ

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Resolvent`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.GaloisResolvent`.

**Statement.** Let K/ℚ be a finite Galois extension and θ ∈ 𝓞_K. Then θ generates a normal integral basis if and only if disc_{K/ℚ}(σθ)_σ = d_K, the discriminant of K. For abelian K this reads Π_χ ⟨θ | χ⟩² = d_K.

**Hypotheses.**

- K/ℚ finite Galois; θ ∈ 𝓞_K.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. If the conjugates are linearly dependent both sides differ (the left side is 0, d_K ≠ 0).
2. Otherwise ℤ[G]θ is a sublattice of 𝓞_K of full rank with disc(σθ) = [𝓞_K : ℤ[G]θ]²·d_K (Algebra.discr_of_matrix_mulVec with the integral change-of-basis matrix, NumberField.discr_eq_discr).
3. The index is 1 exactly when the discriminants agree.
4. The abelian form follows from the resolvent formula.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(√5): θ = (1 + √5)/2 gives 5 = d_K.
- ℚ(i): θ = 1 + i gives −16 = 4·d_K; index 2.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/discriminant-of-normal-basis-resolvent-formula`
- `mathlib:Algebra.discr_of_matrix_mulVec`
- `mathlib:NumberField.discr_eq_discr`
- `mathlib:NumberField.discr`

**Sources.**

- acciaro-normal-integral-bases-2017 — Vincenzo Acciaro, *Computing normal integral bases of abelian number fields*, Section 2 (p. 2).

  > If θ ∈ O, then θ is a normal integral basis generator if and only if the discriminant of the set {g1(θ), . . . , gn(θ)} equals the discriminant of L.

  Verbatim the node; the source states it without proof.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Lemma 4.6 and Remark 4.3 (printed pp. 7–8).

  > Let M and N be a pair of O-lattices in V. Then d(N) = d(M)[M : N]². ... In the case that O = Z and N ⊆ M, we have [M : N]_Z = |M/N|Z.

  The index formula of the proof.

---

### CA.7.D Orders, associated orders, locally free class groups and the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems

Orders in finite-dimensional algebras, left orders and maximal orders; the associated order of a Galois extension, the only order over which 𝓞_L can be free, equal to the group ring exactly in the tame case. The locally free class group of an order is the subgroup of K₀ generated by the classes of locally free lattices; a tame extension defines the Galois module class [𝓞_L]. Fröhlich places it in the kernel group and Taylor proves it has order at most two, vanishing when the group has no symplectic characters; for groups of odd order 𝓞_L is free over ℤ[G]. These last theorems are stated source-scoped: their proofs rest on inputs the atlas does not plan, recorded as gaps.

#### `order-in-finite-dimensional-algebra` — Orders in finite-dimensional algebras

*definition.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Order`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralOrder`.

**Statement.** Let R be a noetherian domain with fraction field F and E a finite-dimensional F-algebra. An R-order in E is an R-subalgebra Λ of E (a subring containing 1 and the image of R) that is an R-lattice in E: finitely generated as an R-module and spanning E over F (Mathlib's Submodule.IsLattice F). Examples: the group ring R[G] in F[G] for a finite group G, the integral closure of R in a finite separable field extension of F, M_n(R) in M_n(F).

**Hypotheses.**

- R noetherian domain, F = Frac R, E a finite-dimensional F-algebra, not necessarily commutative.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define IsOrder Λ as Submodule.IsLattice F applied to the underlying R-submodule of Λ.
2. Every element of an order is integral over R: R[x] ⊆ Λ is a finitely generated R-module.
3. Conversely (E separable: the trace form of the regular representation is nondegenerate) a subring spanning E whose elements are integral is finitely generated: for an F-basis α_i of E inside Λ and β ∈ Λ, the coordinates of β lie in r⁻¹R with r = det(tr(α_iα_j)) (Cramer), so Λ ⊆ r⁻¹Σ Rα_i.
4. Group rings: the images of the group elements form an R-basis of R[G] and an F-basis of F[G].
5. Integral closures: finite over R for separable extensions of the fraction field (Mathlib's IsIntegralClosure.finite) and spanning.

**Acceptance.**

- ℤ[1/2] ⊆ ℚ is a subring spanning ℚ but not an order: it is not finitely generated.
- R itself is an order in F (E = F).

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralOrder.IsOrder` | data | Λ is an R-order in E: an R-subalgebra that is an R-lattice. |
| `IntegralOrder.isOrder_iff` | characterisation | Λ is an order exactly when it is finitely generated over R and spans E over F. |
| `IntegralOrder.IsOrder.isIntegral` | relation | Every element of an order is integral over R. |
| `IntegralOrder.isOrder_of_isIntegral` | constructor | In a separable algebra a spanning subring of integral elements over an integrally closed noetherian R is an order. |
| `IntegralOrder.groupRingSubalgebra` | data | The group ring R[G] as a subalgebra of F[G]. |
| `IntegralOrder.groupRing_isOrder` | example | R[G] is an order in F[G] for a finite group G. |
| `IntegralOrder.integralClosure_isOrder` | compatibility | The integral closure of R in a finite separable field extension of F is an order; for number fields this is 𝓞_K, compatible with Tau Ceti's NumberFieldOrder of the GlobalNumberFields roadmap. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/left-order-of-lattice`: left orders of lattices are orders
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/maximal-order`: maximal orders are orders maximal for inclusion
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order`: the associated order is an order in K[G]
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-class-group`: the locally free class group is defined for orders in semisimple algebras
- Johnston, Section 14: freeness over an order is tested inside a maximal order containing it

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralOrder.order_test_base` (degenerate): R is an order in F.
- `IntegralOrder.order_test_not_finitelyGenerated` (non-example): ℤ[1/2] ⊆ ℚ is not an order.
- `IntegralOrder.order_test_groupRing` (compatibility): ℤ[G] is an order in ℚ[G] for a finite group G.

**Prerequisites.**

- `mathlib:Submodule.IsLattice`
- `mathlib:MonoidAlgebra`
- `mathlib:IsIntegralClosure`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 3.8 and Examples 3.10 (printed p. 5).

  > An R-order in the K-algebra A is a subring Λ of A, having the same unity element of A, and such that Λ is an R-lattice in A.

  Verbatim the definition.

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, Definition 10.2.1 and Lemma 10.3.7 (printed pp. 152–154).

  > An R-order O ⊆ B is an R-lattice that is also a subring of B. ... Let O ⊆ B be a subring of a separable F-algebra B such that OF = B. Then O is an R-order if and only if every α ∈ O is integral.

  The definition and the integrality characterisation in the API.

#### `left-order-of-lattice` — The left order of a lattice

*construction.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Order`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralOrder`.

**Statement.** Let R be a noetherian domain with fraction field F, E a finite-dimensional F-algebra and M an R-lattice in E. The left order of M is O_l(M) = {x ∈ E | x·M ⊆ M}; it is an R-order in E. If M is itself an order then O_l(M) = M.

**Hypotheses.**

- R noetherian; M an R-lattice in E.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. O_l(M) is an R-subalgebra: closed under products and sums and containing R·1.
2. Spanning: for y ∈ E, yM is a lattice in yE and there is nonzero r ∈ R with r·yM ⊆ M, so ry ∈ O_l(M).
3. Finitely generated: there is nonzero s ∈ R with s·1 ∈ M, so O_l(M)·s ⊆ M and O_l(M) ⊆ s⁻¹M, a noetherian module.
4. For an order Λ: Λ·Λ ⊆ Λ gives Λ ⊆ O_l(Λ), and 1 ∈ Λ gives O_l(Λ) ⊆ Λ.

**Acceptance.**

- The left order of 2ℤ ⊆ ℚ is ℤ.
- Rescaling a lattice in a field does not change its left order.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralOrder.leftOrder` | data | The left order {x ∈ E | x·M ⊆ M} of an R-submodule M of E. |
| `IntegralOrder.mem_leftOrder_iff` | characterisation | x ∈ O_l(M) exactly when x·m ∈ M for all m ∈ M. |
| `IntegralOrder.leftOrder_isOrder` | constructor | The left order of a lattice is an order. |
| `IntegralOrder.leftOrder_eq_self` | compatibility | The left order of an order is itself. |
| `IntegralOrder.leftOrder_smul` | relation | In a field, O_l(aM) = O_l(M) for a ≠ 0. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order`: the associated order is the left order of 𝓞_L transported into K[G]
- Johnston, Remark 3.14(iii): the associated order is an order because it is a left order

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralOrder.leftOrder_test_self` (computation): The left order of R ⊆ F is R.
- `IntegralOrder.leftOrder_test_top_contains` (characterisation): Every left order contains R.
- `IntegralOrder.leftOrder_test_scale` (compatibility): The left order of 2M ⊆ ℚ equals that of M.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/order-in-finite-dimensional-algebra`
- `mathlib:Submodule.IsLattice`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 3.11 and Proposition 3.12 (printed p. 5).

  > Let M be any R-lattice in A. Then we define the left order of M in A as A_l(A; M) = {x ∈ A | x · M ⊆ M}. ... Proposition 3.12. Let M be a R-lattice in A. Then A_l(A; M) is an R-order in A.

  Verbatim the node.

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, Lemma 10.2.7 (printed p. 152).

  > O_L(I) ⊆ B is an R-order.

  The same statement with its proof.

#### `maximal-order` — Maximal orders

*definition.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Order`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralOrder`.

**Statement.** Let R be a noetherian domain with fraction field F and E a finite-dimensional F-algebra. An R-order Λ in E is maximal if it is not properly contained in another R-order. The integral closure of an integrally closed R in a finite separable field extension is the unique maximal order there; for a nontrivial finite group G with |G| invertible in F, R[G] is not a maximal order in F[G] when |G| is not a unit in R.

**Hypotheses.**

- R noetherian domain; E finite-dimensional over F.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define IsMaximalOrder as IsOrder together with maximality among orders.
2. Fields: every order consists of integral elements, so lies in the integral closure, which is an order; hence it is the unique maximal order.
3. Group rings: e = |G|⁻¹Σ_g g is an idempotent of F[G], integral over R, commuting with R[G]; R[G][e] is an order strictly containing R[G] when e ∉ R[G], i.e. when |G| is not a unit of R.

**Acceptance.**

- 𝓞_K is the maximal ℤ-order of K; ℤ is the maximal order of ℚ.
- ℤ[G] is not maximal for G ≠ 1.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralOrder.IsMaximalOrder` | data | Λ is a maximal R-order in E. |
| `IntegralOrder.IsMaximalOrder.eq_of_le` | characterisation | An order containing a maximal order equals it. |
| `IntegralOrder.integralClosure_isMaximalOrder` | compatibility | The integral closure of a Dedekind domain in a finite separable field extension is the maximal order. |
| `IntegralOrder.groupRing_not_isMaximalOrder` | other | R[G] is not maximal for G ≠ 1 when |G| is not a unit of R. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/existence-of-maximal-orders`: every order in a separable algebra lies in a maximal order
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-class-group`: the kernel group D(Λ) is the kernel of extension of scalars to a maximal order
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/froehlich-kernel-group`: Fröhlich's theorem puts [𝓞_L] in the kernel group
- Taylor 1981, Section 2: Let 𝔐 be any maximal order in ℚΓ which contains ℤΓ

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralOrder.maximalOrder_test_ringOfIntegers` (compatibility): 𝓞_K is a maximal ℤ-order in K.
- `IntegralOrder.maximalOrder_test_integers` (degenerate): ℤ is the maximal order of ℚ.
- `IntegralOrder.maximalOrder_test_groupRing_not_maximal` (non-example): ℤ[G] is not maximal in ℚ[G] for a nontrivial finite group G.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/order-in-finite-dimensional-algebra`
- `mathlib:IsIntegralClosure`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 13.6 and Examples 13.7 (printed p. 24).

  > We say that Λ is maximal if Λ is maximal with respect to inclusion. ... (i) The ring of integers O_K is the unique maximal Z-order in K. (ii) Then group ring O_K[G] is a maximal O_K-order in K[G] if and only if G is trivial.

  The definition and the two examples used as tests.

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, Definition 10.4.1 (printed p. 155).

  > An R-order O ⊆ B is maximal if it is not properly contained in another R-order.

  The same definition.

#### `existence-of-maximal-orders` — Every order in a separable algebra lies in a maximal order

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Order`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralOrder`.

**Statement.** Let R be an integrally closed noetherian domain with fraction field F and E a separable finite-dimensional F-algebra (nondegenerate trace form). Every R-order in E is contained in a maximal R-order.

**Hypotheses.**

- E separable: the bilinear form (x, y) ↦ tr(xy) of the regular representation is nondegenerate.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Fix an F-basis α_i of E inside Λ; by the Cramer argument of order-in-finite-dimensional-algebra every order containing Λ lies in the finitely generated module r⁻¹ΣRα_i.
2. That module is noetherian, so the nonempty set of orders containing Λ has a maximal element.

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ[G] is separable (characteristic zero, Maschke), so ℤ[G] lies in a maximal order.
- For E = K a number field the maximal order is 𝓞_K.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/maximal-order`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/order-in-finite-dimensional-algebra`

**Sources.**

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, 10.4.2 (printed p. 155).

  > It follows from Lemma 10.3.7 that a separable F-algebra B has a maximal R-order, as follows. By Lemma 10.2.7, B has an R-order O ... so the collection of R-orders containing O is nonempty.

  The node.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 14, first paragraph (printed p. 25).

  > Let A be any O_E-order of full rank in the group algebra A := E[G], and let M be some maximal O_E-order in A containing A.

  The use in the source.

#### `maximal-order-of-cyclic-group-algebra` — The maximal order of ℚ[C_p]

*application.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/Order`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralOrder`.

**Statement.** Let p be a prime and G cyclic of order p. Then ℚ[G] ≅ ℚ × ℚ(ζ_p) by x ↦ (ε(x), α(x)) (augmentation and g ↦ ζ_p); the maximal ℤ-order is M = ℤ[G][e₁] with e₁ = p⁻¹Σ_g g, corresponding to ℤ × ℤ[ζ_p]; and [M : ℤ[G]] = p, with ℤ[G] = {(x, y) ∈ ℤ × ℤ[ζ_p] : x ≡ y mod (1 − ζ_p)}.

**Hypotheses.**

- p prime; G cyclic of order p.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. The map is a surjective ℚ-algebra map between algebras of dimension p, hence an isomorphism.
2. ℤ × ℤ[ζ_p] is the product of the maximal orders of the factors (integral closures), hence the unique maximal order of the commutative algebra.
3. Both components of an element of ℤ[G] reduce to the same element of 𝔽_p = ℤ[ζ_p]/(1 − ζ_p); conversely a count of indices shows equality.
4. e₁ corresponds to (1, 0) and e₂ = 1 − e₁ to (0, 1), so M = ℤ[G][e₁] and the index is p.

**Acceptance.**

- p = 2: ℚ[C_2] ≅ ℚ × ℚ and M = ℤe₁ ⊕ ℤe₋₁, the associated order of ℚ(i)/ℚ.
- The index p shows ℤ[C_p] is not maximal.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/maximal-order`
- `mathlib:IsCyclotomicExtension.Rat.adjoin_singleton_eq_top`
- `mathlib:IsIntegralClosure`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Example 13.9 (printed p. 24).

  > Let M = ϕ^{−1}(Z ⊕ Z[ζ_p]). Then M is the unique maximal Z-order in Q[C_p] and hence contains the group ring Z[C_p]. ... Hence [Z ⊕ Z[ζ_p] : ϕ(Z[C_p])] = [M : Z[C_p]] = p.

  Verbatim the node.

#### `associated-order` — The associated order of a Galois extension

*construction.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/AssociatedOrder`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let A be a Dedekind domain with fraction field K, L/K a finite Galois extension with group G and B the integral closure of A in L. The associated order of L/K is A_{L/K} = {x ∈ K[G] | x·B ⊆ B}, where K[G] acts on L through the Galois representation. It is an A-order in K[G] containing A[G]; by the normal basis map it is the left order of the A-lattice in K[G] corresponding to B. If B is free over some A-order Γ ⊆ K[G] then Γ = A_{L/K}, and A_{L/K} = A[G] exactly when L/K is tamely ramified.

**Hypotheses.**

- A Dedekind domain; B the integral closure of A in the finite Galois extension L of K = Frac A (Mathlib's AKLB setting).

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define the subalgebra by its carrier; closure under products and sums and containment of A are immediate.
2. A[G] ⊆ A_{L/K} since B is G-stable.
3. Order: the normal basis map identifies L with K[G] as K[G]-modules and B with an A-lattice M; A_{L/K} is the left order of M (left-order-of-lattice).

**Acceptance.**

- For L = K, A_{L/K} = A.
- For ℚ(i)/ℚ, A_{L/K} = ℤe₁ ⊕ ℤe₋₁ ⊋ ℤ[G] (associated-order-of-gaussian-integers).

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.associatedOrder` | data | A_{L/K} = {x ∈ K[G] | x·B ⊆ B} as an A-subalgebra of K[G]. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.mem_associatedOrder_iff` | characterisation | x ∈ A_{L/K} exactly when x·b ∈ B for every b ∈ B. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.groupRingSubalgebra_le_associatedOrder` | relation | A[G] ⊆ A_{L/K}. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.associatedOrder_isOrder` | structure | A_{L/K} is an A-order in K[G]. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.associatedOrder_eq_of_free` | characterisation | If B is free of rank one over an A-order Γ ⊆ K[G], then Γ = A_{L/K}. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.associatedOrder_eq_groupRing_iff` | compatibility | A_{L/K} = A[G] exactly when B/A is tamely ramified. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order-eq-group-ring-iff-tame`: tameness is the equality of the associated order with the group ring
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order-of-gaussian-integers`: the wild example
- Johnston, Theorem 1.8 (Leopoldt): the associated order is the order over which 𝓞_K is free for abelian K/ℚ

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralGaloisModule.associatedOrder_test_trivial` (degenerate): For the trivial group, A_{L/K} = A[G] = A.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.associatedOrder_test_contains_groupRing` (characterisation): Every group element lies in A_{L/K}.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.associatedOrder_test_gaussian_strict` (non-example): For ℚ(i)/ℚ, ℤ[G] is strictly smaller than A_{L/K}.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/left-order-of-lattice`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/rational-normal-basis-map`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module`
- `mathlib:IsIntegralClosure`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Definition 3.13 and Remark 3.14 (printed pp. 5–6).

  > We define the associated order of L/K to be A_{L/K} := A_l(K[G]; O_L) = {x ∈ K[G] | xO_L ⊆ O_L}. ... We always have O_K[G] ⊆ A_{L/K}.

  Verbatim the node.

#### `associated-order-of-free` — The only order over which the integers can be free

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/AssociatedOrder`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** In the setting of the associated order: if B = Γ·α is free of rank one over an A-order Γ ⊆ K[G], then Γ = A_{L/K}.

**Hypotheses.**

- Γ an A-order in K[G]; α ∈ B with B = Γ·α freely.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. L = K[G]·α freely (tensor with K).
2. x ∈ A_{L/K}: xα ∈ B = Γα, so xα = yα with y ∈ Γ, and freeness gives x = y ∈ Γ.
3. γ ∈ Γ: γB = γΓα ⊆ Γα = B, so γ ∈ A_{L/K}.

**Acceptance.**

- A normal integral basis gives A_{L/K} = A[G].
- For ℚ(i)/ℚ, ℤ[i] = A_{L/K}·(1 + i).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proposition 3.15 (printed p. 6).

  > Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of number fields or p-adic fields with Galois group G. If O_L is free over an O_K-order Γ in K[G], then Γ = A_{L/K}.

  Verbatim the node.

#### `associated-order-eq-group-ring-iff-tame` — The associated order is the group ring exactly in the tame case

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/AssociatedOrder`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite Galois extension of number fields (or of nonarchimedean local fields) with group G. Then A_{L/K} = 𝓞_K[G] if and only if L/K is tamely ramified.

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois; number fields or local fields.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Wild ⇒ strict: the trace ideal t = Tr(𝓞_L) is a proper nonzero ideal (tame-iff-trace-surjective); pick a ∈ t⁻¹ ∖ 𝓞_K; then a·Σ_g g maps 𝓞_L into a·t ⊆ 𝓞_K, so lies in A_{L/K} but not in 𝓞_K[G].
2. Tame ⇒ equality, local case: 𝒪_L is free over 𝒪_K[G] (local-noether), so A_{L/K} = 𝒪_K[G] (associated-order-of-free).
3. Tame ⇒ equality, global case: at every v the completed module is free over 𝒪̂_v[G] (locally-free-of-tame); the completion of A_{L/K} stabilises it, so lies in 𝒪̂_v[G] by associated-order-of-free; hence 𝓞_K[G] ⊆ A_{L/K} have equal completions everywhere and are equal (sublattice-eq-of-completions).

**Acceptance.**

- ℚ(i)/ℚ: A_{L/K} ⊋ ℤ[G].
- ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ: A_{L/K} = ℤ[G].

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order-of-free`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/tame-iff-trace-surjective`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/local-noether`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-of-tame`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/sublattice-eq-of-completions`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Proposition 9.9 (printed pp. 18–19).

  > Let K be a p-adic field and let L/K be a finite Galois extension with group G. Let A_{L/K} = {x ∈ K[G] | xO_L ⊆ O_L} be the associated order. Then A_{L/K} = O_K[G] if and only if L/K is tamely ramified.

  The local case, verbatim.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Remark 9.11 (printed p. 19).

  > The analogue of Proposition 9.9 holds for number fields. However, the analogue of Corollary 9.10 does not hold for number fields: tameness is a necessary but not a sufficient condition for the existence of a normal integral basis.

  The global case, which the source asserts without proof; the proof steps supply it.

#### `associated-order-of-gaussian-integers` — The associated order of ℚ(i)/ℚ

*application.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/AssociatedOrder`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** For L = ℚ(i) with G = {1, σ}: e₁ = (1 + σ)/2 and e₋₁ = (1 − σ)/2 lie in A_{L/ℚ}; A_{L/ℚ} = ℤe₁ ⊕ ℤe₋₁, the maximal order of ℚ[G]; and ℤ[i] = A_{L/ℚ}·(1 + i) since e₁(1 + i) = 1 and e₋₁(1 + i) = i.

**Hypotheses.**

- L = ℚ(i) = ℚ(ζ_4).

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. e₁(a + bi) = a and e₋₁(a + bi) = bi, so both preserve ℤ[i].
2. ℤe₁ ⊕ ℤe₋₁ is the maximal order of ℚ[C_2] (maximal-order-of-cyclic-group-algebra with p = 2), so it equals A_{L/ℚ}.
3. The computation e₁(1 + i) = 1, e₋₁(1 + i) = i shows ℤ[i] is free over it with basis 1 + i; associated-order-of-free confirms the equality.

**Acceptance.**

- A wild extension with an associated order strictly larger than the group ring.
- ℤ[i] is free over its associated order although it has no normal integral basis.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/associated-order-of-free`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/maximal-order-of-cyclic-group-algebra`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Example 3.16 (printed p. 6).

  > Let e_1 = (1+σ)/2 and e_{−1} = (1−σ)/2. Then e_1 and e_{−1} map Z[i] to Z[i], so are in the associated order A_{Q(i)/Q}. ... Moreover, Z[i] is a A_{Q(i)/Q}-module with basis α = 1+i since e_1α = 1 and e_{−1}α = i.

  Verbatim the node.

#### `locally-free-class-group` — The locally free class group of an order

*construction.* **Planet:** *Locally free class group*. Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/ClassGroup`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.LocallyFree`.

**Statement.** Let A be a Dedekind domain with fraction field K and Λ an A-order in a finite-dimensional semisimple K-algebra (for instance 𝓞_K[G] ⊆ K[G], or ℤ[G] ⊆ ℚ[G]). Let K₀(Λ) be the Grothendieck group of finitely generated projective Λ-modules (Tau Ceti's split K₀, with the ring-level interface of KTheoryLowDegrees Z.1). The locally free class group Cl(Λ) is the subgroup of K₀(Λ) generated by the classes [M] − n[Λ] of the locally free Λ-lattices M of rank n (projective by locally-free-lattice-is-projective). [M] − n[Λ] = 0 exactly when M is stably free: M ⊕ Λ^k ≅ Λ^{n+k}. For Λ = A, Cl(A) is the ideal class group. For an order Λ′ ⊇ Λ (a maximal order), extension of scalars gives Cl(Λ) → Cl(Λ′), whose kernel is the kernel group D(Λ).

**Hypotheses.**

- A Dedekind domain; Λ module-finite over A.
- K₀ is the split Grothendieck group of finitely generated projective left Λ-modules.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define Cl(Λ) as the additive subgroup generated by the classes; the class of a locally free lattice of rank n is [M] − n[Λ].
2. Direct sums: [M ⊕ N] − (n + m)[Λ] is the sum of the two classes (SplitK0 additivity).
3. Stable freeness: equality of classes in K₀ is stable isomorphism (KTheoryLowDegrees Z.1).
4. Every element is the class of a locally free lattice of rank one (Reiner's theorem, locally-free-class-represented-by-rank-one).
5. Λ = A: locally free lattices are the finitely generated projective modules of constant rank, and K₀(A) ≅ ℤ ⊕ Pic(A) (KTheoryLowDegrees Z.4); the rank-zero part is Pic(A) = Cl(A).
6. Extension of scalars along Λ → Λ′ maps locally free lattices to locally free lattices of the same rank (KTheoryLowDegrees Z.1 scalar extension).

**Acceptance.**

- Cl(ℤ) = 0; Cl(ℤ[√−5]) ≅ ℤ/2.
- Cl(ℤ[C_2]) = 0, although ℤ[C_2] is not a maximal order.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `LocallyFree.classGroup` | data | Cl(Λ) ⊆ K₀(Λ), generated by the classes of locally free lattices. |
| `LocallyFree.classOf` | constructor | The class [M] − n[Λ] of a locally free lattice of rank n. |
| `LocallyFree.classOf_eq_zero_iff` | characterisation | The class vanishes exactly when M ⊕ Λ^k ≅ Λ^{n+k} for some k. |
| `LocallyFree.classOf_prod` | simp | The class of a direct sum is the sum of the classes. |
| `LocallyFree.classOf_free` | simp | The class of Λ^n is zero. |
| `LocallyFree.exists_classOf_eq` | characterisation | Every element is the class of a locally free lattice of rank one. |
| `LocallyFree.classGroupMap` | functoriality | Extension of scalars along a morphism of A-orders. |
| `LocallyFree.kernelGroup` | data | The kernel group D(Λ) = ker(Cl(Λ) → Cl(Λ′)) for an order Λ′ ⊇ Λ. |
| `LocallyFree.classGroupSelfEquiv` | compatibility | Cl(A) ≅ the ideal class group of A. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-class`: the Galois module class of a tame extension lies here
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/taylor-class-of-order-two`: Taylor's theorem is a statement in Cl(ℤΓ)
- Ferri–Greither, Introduction: realisable classes and the Stickelberger ideal act on Cl(𝓞_K[G])

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `LocallyFree.classGroup_test_integers` (degenerate): Cl(ℤ) is trivial.
- `LocallyFree.classGroup_test_ideal` (compatibility): The class of a nonzero nonprincipal ideal of A in Cl(A) is nonzero.
- `LocallyFree.classGroup_test_cyclicTwo` (computation): Cl(ℤ[C_2]) is trivial.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice-is-projective`
- `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitK0`
- `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitK0.of`
- `tauceti:TauCeti.SplitK0.of_biprod`
- `tauceti:TauCeti.finiteProjectiveModules`
- `KTheoryLowDegrees:Z.1`
- `KTheoryLowDegrees:Z.4/rank-pic-equivalence`
- `mathlib:ClassGroup`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 15.1 (printed pp. 27–28).

  > We now define the (locally free) class group ClΛ as the additive abelian group generated by the classes [M] of locally free Λ-lattices M, with addition defined by (16). Note that the identity element of ClΛ is [Λ], the class of all stably free lattices.

  The node, with Reiner's classes identified with [M] − n[Λ] in K₀.

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, Remark 20.7.13 (printed p. 328).

  > Then for P, Q ∈ P(O), we have [P] = [Q] ∈ K_0(O) if and only if P, Q are stably isomorphic [CR87, Proposition 38.22]. ... StCl O ∼→ SK_0(O), [I]_St ↦ [I] − [O].

  The identification of the stable class group with a subgroup of K₀.

- ferri-greither-tame-galois-2019 — Fabio Ferri and Cornelius Greither, *Tame Galois module structure revisited*, Introduction (pp. 2–3).

  > We recall that there is the following surjection, with kernel denoted by D(O_K[G]): 0 −→ D(O_K[G]) −→ Cl(O_K[G]) −→ Cl(M) −→ 0.

  The kernel group.

#### `locally-free-class-represented-by-rank-one` — Every locally free class is represented by a locally free ideal

*lemma.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/ClassGroup`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.LocallyFree`.

**Statement.** Let Λ be an A-order in a finite-dimensional semisimple K-algebra, A a Dedekind domain. For locally free Λ-lattices M, M′ there are t ≥ 0 and a locally free Λ-lattice M″ of rank one with M ⊕ M′ ≅ Λ^t ⊕ M″. Hence every element of Cl(Λ) is [M″] − [Λ] for a locally free lattice M″ of rank one.

**Hypotheses.**

- A Dedekind domain with fraction field a global field for the finiteness statements; semisimple ambient algebra.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Reduce to rank one: a locally free lattice of rank n is Λ^{n−1} ⊕ (rank one) up to isomorphism (Reiner Theorem 27.4).
2. Rank one sums: by weak approximation move one locally free ideal to be locally trivial at the primes where the other is not; the sum map to Λ is then locally surjective, hence surjective and split, with locally free kernel (the argument of Voight Proposition 20.7.4 for simple algebras).

**Acceptance.**

- For Λ = A this is the Steinitz identity I ⊕ J ≅ A ⊕ IJ (KTheoryLowDegrees Z.4).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-class-group`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-lattice`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 15.1, (15) (printed p. 28).

  > Given two locally free lattices M and M′, it can be shown that (15) M ∔ M′ ≅ Λ^(t) ∔ M″ for some locally free ideal M″ and some t ∈ Z≥0 (see [Rei03, Theorem (27.4)]).

  The node; the source cites Reiner without proof.

- voight-quaternion-2021 — John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras*, Proposition 20.7.4 (printed p. 326).

  > If I, I′ ⊆ B are locally principal left fractional O-ideals, then there exists a locally principal left fractional O-ideal J and an isomorphism I ⊕ I′ ≃ J ⊕ O of left O-modules.

  The rank-one case for simple B, with proof.

#### `ring-of-integers-class` — The Galois module class of the ring of integers

*construction.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/ClassGroup`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let L/K be a finite tamely ramified Galois extension of number fields with group G. The Galois module class of L/K is [𝓞_L] = [𝓞_L] − [𝓞_K[G]] ∈ Cl(𝓞_K[G]) (𝓞_L being locally free of rank one by Noether), and its restriction [𝓞_L]_ℤ = [𝓞_L] − [K:ℚ][ℤ[G]] ∈ Cl(ℤ[G]) (𝓞_L locally free of rank [K : ℚ] over ℤ[G]). The class is zero when L/K has a normal integral basis, vanishes exactly when 𝓞_L is stably free, and is killed by the augmentation Cl(𝓞_K[G]) → Cl(𝓞_K).

**Hypotheses.**

- L/K finite Galois and tame.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Define the two classes with classOf applied to locally-free-of-tame and ring-of-integers-locally-free-over-integral-group-ring.
2. Normal integral basis: 𝓞_L ≅ 𝓞_K[G], so the class is zero.
3. Augmentation: 𝓞_K ⊗_{𝓞_K[G]} 𝓞_L is the module of coinvariants of the projective module 𝓞_L. For the free module 𝓞_K[G] the norm map from coinvariants to invariants is the isomorphism 𝓞_K[G]/I_G ≅ 𝓞_K·N (x ↦ ε(x)N), so it is an isomorphism for every direct summand of a free module, in particular for 𝓞_L, whose invariants are 𝓞_K (the same argument as trace-surjective-of-projective); so the image class is [𝓞_K] − [𝓞_K] = 0.

**Acceptance.**

- L = K: zero.
- ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ: zero (normal integral basis).
- Defined only for tame L/K: for ℚ(i)/ℚ there is no class.

**API.** What a user of this object needs in order to use it without unfolding the definition.

| name | role | statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.ringOfIntegersClass` | constructor | [𝓞_L] ∈ Cl(𝓞_K[G]) for tame L/K. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.ringOfIntegersClassInt` | constructor | [𝓞_L]_ℤ ∈ Cl(ℤ[G]). |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.ringOfIntegersClass_eq_zero_of_hasNIB` | relation | A normal integral basis makes the class zero. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.ringOfIntegersClass_eq_zero_iff` | characterisation | The class is zero exactly when 𝓞_L is stably free over 𝓞_K[G]. |
| `IntegralGaloisModule.classGroupMap_augmentation_ringOfIntegersClass` | relation | The augmentation kills the class. |

**Uses.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/taylor-class-of-order-two`: Taylor computes [𝓞_L]_ℤ
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/froehlich-kernel-group`: Fröhlich places [𝓞_L]_ℤ in the kernel group
- Johnston, Section 15.5: realisable classes R(𝓞_K[G]) are the classes of tame G-extensions

**Unit tests.** A plausible wrong definition fails one of these.

- `IntegralGaloisModule.ringOfIntegersClass_test_trivial` (degenerate): For L = K the class is zero.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.ringOfIntegersClass_test_cyclotomic` (computation): For ℚ(ζ_p)/ℚ (p odd) the class is zero.
- `IntegralGaloisModule.ringOfIntegersClass_test_intZero` (compatibility): Over K = ℚ a normal integral basis makes the integral class zero.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-class-group`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-of-tame`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-locally-free-over-integral-group-ring`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/normal-integral-basis`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/trace-surjective-of-projective`

**Sources.**

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 15.1 (printed p. 28).

  > Now let L/K be a finite tamely ramified Galois extension of number fields with Galois group G. Recall from Theorem 10.7 that O_L is a locally free O_K[G]-module of rank 1. Hence we can consider the class [O_L] in Cl(O_K[G]).

  Verbatim the node.

- ferri-greither-tame-galois-2019 — Fabio Ferri and Cornelius Greither, *Tame Galois module structure revisited*, Introduction, McCulloh's theorem (p. 3).

  > ε∗: Cl(O_K[G]) −→ Cl(O_K), [M] ↦ [O_K ⊗_{O_K[G]} M] ... We define Cl^0(O_K[G]) to be the kernel of the above homomorphism.

  The augmentation map; McCulloh's description R(O_K[G]) = Cl^0(O_K[G])^J places realisable classes in its kernel.

#### `froehlich-kernel-group` — Fröhlich: the Galois module class lies in the kernel group

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/ClassGroup`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let M/N be a tame Galois extension of number fields with group Γ, and 𝔐 a maximal order of ℚΓ containing ℤΓ. Then [𝓞_M]_ℤ ∈ D(ℤΓ) = ker(Cl(ℤΓ) → Cl(𝔐)): 𝓞_M becomes stably free over 𝔐.

**Hypotheses.**

- M/N tame Galois; 𝔐 ⊇ ℤΓ maximal.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Fröhlich's proof (Appendix of his book, cited by Taylor) is not decomposed here; it runs through Fröhlich's Hom-description of Cl(ℤΓ) and the resolvend of a local normal integral basis generator. See the gap on the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems.

**Acceptance.**

- For Γ abelian, Cl(𝔐) is a product of ideal class groups of cyclotomic fields.

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-class`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/maximal-order`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-class-group`

**Sources.**

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Section 2 (printed p. 44).

  > In [F5], Fröhlich showed that (𝔒_M) ∈ D(ℤΓ), i.e. 𝔒_M becomes stably free over 𝔐.

  Verbatim the node; the proof is in Fröhlich's work, not in the sources read.

#### `taylor-class-of-order-two` — Taylor's theorem: the Galois module class has order at most two

*theorem.* **Planet:** *Taylor's theorem (Fröhlich's conjecture)*. Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/ClassGroup`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let M/N be a tame Galois extension of number fields with group Γ. Then 2·[𝓞_M]_ℤ = 0 in Cl(ℤΓ). (Taylor's Theorem 1: [𝓞_M]_ℤ = t(W), the Cassou-Noguès–Fröhlich root number class, whose values are signs of Artin root numbers of symplectic characters.)

**Hypotheses.**

- M/N tame Galois.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. Taylor's proof (Fröhlich's Hom-description (2.1), the representative u = Det(A)·W′·τ_Γ⁻¹ of the class, Galois Gauss sums and the group logarithm, Sections 2–12 of Taylor 1981) is not decomposed here; see the gap on the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems.

**Acceptance.**

- For Γ abelian the class is zero (Hilbert–Speiser over ℚ; for general N, Taylor's theorem with no symplectic characters).
- Martinet's quaternion example: for Γ = Q₈ and N = ℚ the class can be nonzero, so "order two" cannot be improved to "zero".

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-class`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/locally-free-class-group`

**Sources.**

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Theorem 1 (printed p. 41).

  > Theorem 1. (𝔒_M) = t(W), and so in particular (a) as the Artin root numbers of sympletic characters are ±1, (𝔒_M)² = 1.

  The node is part (a); the source writes Cl(ℤΓ) multiplicatively.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Theorem 15.3 (printed p. 29).

  > Let L/K be a tame Galois extension of number fields with group G. Then [O_L]² is trivial in Cl(Z[G]).

  The same statement.

#### `taylor-no-symplectic-stably-free` — Taylor: without symplectic characters the ring of integers is stably free

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/ClassGroup`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let M/N be a tame Galois extension of number fields with group Γ. If Γ has no irreducible symplectic (quaternionic) character, that is no irreducible complex representation carries a nonzero Γ-invariant alternating form (Frobenius–Schur indicator −1), then [𝓞_M]_ℤ = 0: 𝓞_M is stably free over ℤΓ.

**Hypotheses.**

- M/N tame Galois; every irreducible character of Γ has Frobenius–Schur indicator 0 or 1.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. t(W) is defined from the Artin root numbers of the irreducible symplectic characters only; with none, t(W) = 1 (Taylor Section 2).
2. Apply Taylor's Theorem 1 (see the gap on the Fröhlich–Taylor theorems).
3. The characterisation of symplectic characters by invariant alternating forms is the Frobenius–Schur trichotomy (CharacterTheory Layer 7).

**Acceptance.**

- Abelian, dihedral, symmetric groups and groups of odd order have no symplectic characters.
- Q₈ has one (its two-dimensional representation).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/taylor-class-of-order-two`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-class`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CharacterTheory#layer-7-the-frobenius-schur-indicator-and-realquaternionic-type` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Theorem 1(b) (printed p. 41).

  > (b) the only obstructions to the vanishing of the class of 𝔒_M, are the signs of the Artin root numbers of symplectic characters.

  The node is the case with no symplectic characters.

- johnston-galois-modules-2016 — Henri Johnston, *Notes on Galois Modules*, Section 15.4 (printed p. 29).

  > We say G satisfies condition ⋆ if D_i ≠ H for each i (this is non-standard terminology!) Note that condition ⋆ is equivalent to G having no irreducible symplectic characters.

  The hypothesis.

#### `taylor-odd-order-free` — Taylor: for groups of odd order the ring of integers is free over ℤΓ

*theorem.* Module `TauCeti/NumberTheory/GaloisModule/ClassGroup`, namespace `TauCeti.ClassicalArithmetic.IntegralGaloisModule`.

**Statement.** Let M/N be a tame Galois extension of number fields with group Γ of odd order. Then 𝓞_M is a free ℤΓ-module of rank [N : ℚ].

**Hypotheses.**

- M/N tame Galois; |Γ| odd.

**Construction, or proof, in steps.**

1. A group of odd order has no nontrivial real-valued irreducible character (CharacterTheory Layer 7: real characters count real classes, and an odd-order group has only the identity as a real class), hence no symplectic character; so [𝓞_M]_ℤ = 0 (taylor-no-symplectic-stably-free).
2. ℚΓ satisfies the Eichler condition (no totally definite quaternion component), so ℤΓ has locally free cancellation (Jacobinski's cancellation theorem; see the gap).
3. Stably free and locally free of rank [N : ℚ] with cancellation gives free of that rank.

**Acceptance.**

- Γ cyclic of odd order: consistent with Hilbert–Speiser when N = ℚ.
- The hypothesis "odd order" cannot be dropped: Cougnard's example over Q₃₂ is stably free but not free (quoted by Johnston).

**Prerequisites.**

- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/taylor-no-symplectic-stably-free`
- `ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.7/ring-of-integers-locally-free-over-integral-group-ring`
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CharacterTheory#layer-7-the-frobenius-schur-indicator-and-realquaternionic-type` (Tau Ceti roadmap layer)

**Sources.**

- taylor-froehlich-conjecture-1981 — M. J. Taylor, *On Fröhlich's conjecture for rings of integers of tame extensions*, Corollary and its proof (printed pp. 41–42).

  > Corollary. If further |Γ| is odd then 𝔒_M is a free ℤΓ-module. Proof. As Γ is of odd order, it has no irreducible sympletic characters (cf. 8.1 of [S]). Thus we deduce firstly that (𝔒_M) = 1 and secondly that the group algebra ℚΓ satisfies the Eichler condition

  Verbatim the node; the rank is [N : ℚ] by local freeness.

---

### CA.7 Dependencies

- Inside this roadmap: CA.7 uses no node of CA.0–CA.6. The stage input CA.5 (number-field arithmetic handoff) is honoured through the same Tau Ceti owners CA.5 points to: NumberFieldArithmetic for completions and ramification, and the pinned Mathlib number-field library.
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-5-the-global-local-dictionary-at-finite-places`: integral semilocal equivalence, decomposition groups as local Galois groups, invariant matching.
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-2-unramified-extensions-and-frobenius` and `#layer-3-ramification-the-tame-and-wild-cases-and-the-filtration`: unramified extensions and their Galois groups; the structure of tame totally ramified extensions, the Galois criterion and Eisenstein power bases.
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-13-norm-theorems-and-class-fields`: `kroneckerWeber`.
- `tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CharacterTheory#layer-7-the-frobenius-schur-indicator-and-realquaternionic-type`: the Frobenius–Schur trichotomy, symplectic characters, groups of odd order.
- `KTheoryLowDegrees:Z.1`: ring K₀ for noncommutative rings, stable equality, scalar extension and restriction along finite projective ring maps; `KTheoryLowDegrees:Z.4/rank-pic-equivalence`: K₀ of a Dedekind domain.
- The Tau Ceti roadmap GrothendieckEulerForms (Layers 0–2) supplies the split K₀ that Z.1 instantiates; its built declarations TauCeti.SplitK0 and TauCeti.finiteProjectiveModules are cited directly.
- Consumers: ComputationalNumberTheory CN.2 (algorithms for normal integral bases and freeness over orders) consumes these objects.

### CA.7 Acceptance tests

- *A rational normal basis need not be integral; wild ramification is the obstruction.* `gaussian-integers-have-no-normal-integral-basis`: 1 + i generates a normal basis of ℚ(i)/ℚ, ℚ(i)/ℚ has no normal integral basis, 2 is wildly ramified, Tr(ℤ[i]) = 2ℤ; its associated order is strictly larger than ℤ[G] (`associated-order-of-gaussian-integers`), and ℤ[i] is not locally free over ℤ[C₂] (unit test `LocallyFree.test_gaussian_not_locallyFree`).
- *Local freeness is separate from global freeness.* `locally-free-not-free-example`: a nonprincipal ideal is locally free of rank one and not free; its class in Cl(A) is nonzero (unit test `LocallyFree.classGroup_test_ideal`). For group rings, [𝓞_L] = 0 means stably free, and freeness needs cancellation (`taylor-odd-order-free`).
- *Noether.* `local-noether` and `noether-theorem`: tame ⇔ local normal integral basis ⇔ locally free of rank one ⇔ projective, with ℚ_p(ζ_p)/ℚ_p and ℚ_2(i)/ℚ_2 as the positive and negative examples.
- *Hilbert–Speiser.* `hilbert-speiser`: ℚ(√5), ℚ(√−3), ℚ(ζ_7) have normal integral bases generated by Gauss periods; ℚ(i), ℚ(√2), ℚ(√−2) do not.
- *Resolvents and discriminants.* `discriminant-of-normal-basis-resolvent-formula` and `normal-integral-basis-discriminant-criterion`: for ℚ(√5) and θ = (1 + √5)/2 the squared resolvents multiply to 5 = d_K; for ℚ(i) and θ = 1 + i they give −16 = 4·d_K.
- *Orders.* ℤ[C_p] has index p in its maximal order (`maximal-order-of-cyclic-group-algebra`); ℤ[1/2] is not an order; the associated order of a tame extension is the group ring.

---

## Gaps

Each of these is something this packet could not establish from the sources read. None is papered over,
and every node that depends on one names it.

### The job names a retired roadmap as a supplier

The issue for this job names BP-FoundationsAndLibraryIntegration among its suppliers. That roadmap was retired on 16 September 2026 and is listed in data/roadmap-retirements.json, and the blueprint checker warns on any prerequisite naming one of its stages. No node of this packet names it, and the targets it would have supplied - the elementary arithmetic that CA.0 rests on - are cited directly to the pinned libraries instead. This is recorded here so that the maintainer can see that the supplier list of this job is stale rather than that the supplier was ignored.

### The two inputs of the Eisenstein reciprocity proof besides Stickelberger's theorem have no owner

The proof of Eisenstein reciprocity in the source read (Adhikari §4) rests on three inputs it does not prove. The first, Stickelberger's theorem (Φ(P)) = P^γ, γ = Σ t σ_t^(-1) (Proposition 4.3), is now planned by FiniteFieldsAndCharacterSums FF.1 as FiniteFieldsAndCharacterSums:FF.1/stickelberger-relation, which the Eisenstein reciprocity node cites. The other two have no owner: ε(α) = ±1 for primary α (Proposition 4.6, cited to Ireland–Rosen), and (Φ(P) / P')_l = (N P' / P)_l for primes with coprime norms (cited to Ireland–Rosen). A public source for them exists in the same proceedings (S. A. Katre, 'Gauss-Jacobi sums and Stickelberger's theorem', bprim.org, jacn.pdf), not read here. Until an owner plans these two statements, the Eisenstein reciprocity node rests on them as a recorded gap.

Needed by: `CA.1/eisenstein-reciprocity`.

### Catalan–Mihăilescu has no owner in the atlas

The reviewed audit lists Catalan–Mihăilescu (xᵖ − y^q = 1 has only 3² − 2³ = 1 for x, y, p, q ≥ 2) among CA.4's source-scoped exponential equations. Its only proof (Mihăilescu 2002–2004; expositions by Bilu and by Schoof) uses the arithmetic of cyclotomic fields: the Stickelberger ideal, Thaine's theorem and cyclotomic units, not elementary methods, so it is outside the elementary routes that RS-03 keeps in CA.4. No layer of the atlas plans it: DiophantineApproximationAndTranscendence:DT.4 plans only Tijdeman's effective bound (node DT.4/tijdeman-catalan-bound), and the cyclotomic inputs live in IntegralIwasawaTheory (L0, I.3, I.6). CA.4 plans the elementary instances it owns: Levi ben Gershon's theorem (powers of 2 and 3, node powers-of-two-and-three-differing-by-one) and, through Leonetti's survey, the cases whose proofs are elementary.
