The maintainer should route Mihăilescu's theorem to a cyclotomic owner (a Part II of IntegralIwasawaTheory, or a new roadmap); CA.4 then imports it only as a comparison.

Needed by: `CA.4`.

### The analytic core of Smyth's nonreciprocal bound has no public source

Smyth's theorem (M(α) ≥ θ₀ for a nonzero nonreciprocal algebraic integer) is stated in Smyth's survey (15), printed p. 328, with an outline: the integer power series F = P(0)P/P* (CA.6/smyth-integer-power-series), its factorisation F = f/g into functions bounded by 1 on the unit disc with f(0) = g(0) = M(P)⁻¹, and Schur's characterisation of the coefficients of such functions. The step turning these into the constant θ₀ is in Smyth, On the product of the conjugates outside the unit circle of an algebraic integer, Bull. London Math. Soc. 3 (1971), 169-175, which is not served publicly (the publisher's pages answer automated requests with a challenge page and the author's paper list does not include it); no other public source reproducing the argument was found (searched: arXiv, the author's page, the expositions of Borwein-Hare-Mossinghoff and Saunders, which reach only (1 + √17)/4). Schur's coefficient theorem for bounded analytic functions is also absent from Mathlib and planned by no roadmap. The node carries the outline and the gap; Siegel's theorem on the smallest Pisot number inherits it.

Needed by: `CA.6/smyth-nonreciprocal-lower-bound`, `CA.6/plastic-number-least-pisot`.

### Transfinite diameter, Dubinin's hedgehog theorem and Bertrandias's rationality theorem are planned by no roadmap

Dimitrov's proof of the Schinzel-Zassenhaus conjecture (arXiv:1912.12545v1, pp. 12-16) uses three inputs from potential theory: the transfinite diameter (logarithmic capacity) of a compact subset of ℂ; Dubinin's theorem (Theorem 3) that the hedgehog K(a₁, …, a_n), the union of the segments [0, a_i], has transfinite diameter at most (max|a_i|^n/4)^{1/n}; and Bertrandias's rationality theorem (Theorem 4, Corollary 5), that a power series in ℤ[[1/X]] continuing analytically to the complement of a compact set of transfinite diameter < 1 is a rational function. Mathlib has none of these, the atlas has no roadmap for logarithmic capacity or for Pólya-Carlson-Bertrandias rationality criteria (DT.5 plans G-functions but not this criterion), and the source cites Dubinin's book and Amice's 'Les nombres p-adiques' (not public) for the proofs, giving only an outline of Bertrandias's argument (Hankel determinants bounded through Chebyshev polynomials of the compact set). The arithmetic half of the proof is planned here (CA.6/root-power-polynomial-congruence, CA.6/integral-square-root-power-series, CA.6/root-power-polynomial-square-cyclotomic); the analytic half needs an owner, which the restructure list proposes.

Needed by: `CA.6/schinzel-zassenhaus-dimitrov`.

### The Fröhlich–Taylor theorems are stated, not decomposed

Taylor's Theorem 1 ([𝓞_M] = t(W) in Cl(ℤΓ)) and Fröhlich's membership [𝓞_M] ∈ D(ℤΓ) are stated from Taylor 1981 (read for its statements, pp. 41–45) and Johnston Section 15. Their proofs need Fröhlich's Hom-description of Cl(ℤΓ) (Taylor (2.1)), the Cassou-Noguès–Fröhlich root number class t(W), Galois Gauss sums of Artin characters and Taylor's group logarithm (Taylor Sections 3–12), none of which is planned by any roadmap of the atlas and whose standard reference (Fröhlich, Galois module structure of algebraic integers) is not freely available. A roadmap owning Artin root numbers and Galois Gauss sums would have to supply these before the proofs can be decomposed.

Needed by: `CA.7/froehlich-kernel-group`, `CA.7/taylor-class-of-order-two`, `CA.7/taylor-no-symplectic-stably-free`.

### Jacobinski's cancellation theorem has no public source with proof and no owner

The freeness corollary for groups of odd order passes from stably free to free by Jacobinski's cancellation theorem (an order in a semisimple algebra satisfying the Eichler condition has locally free cancellation; Curtis–Reiner (51.24), cited by Johnston 15.3 and Taylor p. 42). No free source proving it was found (Voight Section 20.7 proves the quaternion case through Eichler's theorem only), and no roadmap of the atlas plans it.

Needed by: `CA.7/taylor-odd-order-free`.

### Reiner's theorem that locally free classes are represented by locally free ideals has a public proof only for simple algebras

Johnston (15) cites Reiner, Maximal Orders, Theorem 27.4 (not freely available). Voight Proposition 20.7.4 proves the rank-one statement for orders in simple algebras, by weak approximation and Krull–Schmidt; group algebras of nontrivial groups are not simple. The semisimple case needs the same argument componentwise together with the reduction from rank n to rank one, and its public source is missing.

Needed by: `CA.7/locally-free-class-represented-by-rank-one`.

- **FiniteFieldsAndCharacterSums:FF.1** — For a finite field F (in particular the residue field 𝓞_K/𝔭 of a number field) and multiplicative characters χ of F with values in a domain (in particular the power residue character with values in 𝓞_K), the comparison between Mathlib's convention, in which every MulChar, including the trivial one, vanishes at 0 (so jacobiSum 1 1 = q - 2 and jacobiSum 1 χ = -1), and the classical convention ε(0) = 1 for the trivial character used in the reciprocity sources (J(ε, ε) = q, J(ε, χ) = 0); the transport of Gauss and Jacobi sums along a ring embedding of the value ring (𝓞_K → ℂ, MulChar.ringHomComp) with g(χ, ψ) = Σ_t χ(t) ψ(t) for the canonical trace additive character ψ; and the shift identity g_c(χ) = χ(c)^(-1) g(χ) for c ≠ 0. CA.1 imports these normalisations and does not restate them. This is K2SymbolsBrauer:T.7/classical-local-symbols' normResidueSymbol with its orientation pinned; CA.1 uses it and does not define a second symbol. - **QSeriesPartitionsAndMockModularForms:QM.0** — The Jacobi triple product identity as an identity of formal power series in x with coefficients Laurent polynomials in a: ∏_{n≥1}(1 + a x^{2n−1})(1 + a⁻¹x^{2n−1})(1 − x^{2n}) = Σ_{n∈ℤ} aⁿx^{n²}; together with its specialisations in ℤ[[x]] used by Hirschhorn: (i) Jacobi's identity ∏_{n≥1}(1 − xⁿ)³ = ½Σ_{n∈ℤ}(−1)ⁿ(2n + 1)x^{(n²+n)/2} (Hardy–Wright, Theorem 357); (ii) ∏_{n≥1}(1 + x^{2n−1})²(1 − x^{2n}) = Σ_{n∈ℤ}x^{n²}; (iii) 2∏_{n≥1}(1 + x^{2n})²(1 − x^{2n}) = Σ_{r∈ℤ}x^{r²+r}; (iv) Gauss's identity ∏_{n≥1}(1 − xⁿ)/(1 + xⁿ) = Σ_{n∈ℤ}(−1)ⁿx^{n²}. Infinite products are limits in the x-adic topology (coefficientwise finite). Needed by: `CA.4/hirschhorn-sixth-power-identity`, `CA.4/hirschhorn-logarithmic-derivative-identity`, `CA.4/theta-fourth-power-lambert-series`.
- **ComputationalNumberTheory:CN.2** — Certified outputs, for a number field K given by a defining polynomial, in the carriers CA.5 interprets: (a) an integral basis as a Module.Basis ℤ (𝓞 K) with its multiplication table and a certificate that its discriminant equals NumberField.discr K (or is squarefree); (b) ideals as Hermite matrices in that basis (CA.3 convention) with a proof that the column lattice is 𝓞_K-stable; (c) a family of nonzero prime ideals containing every prime of absolute norm at most the Minkowski bound, a relation matrix whose columns are relations, and a completeness certificate that the columns span the relation lattice (for instance through a certified lower bound h* ≤ h_K R_K with |det| · R(u) < 2h*); (d) a unit family of unit-rank length with IsMaxRank and its regulator with certified error. A terminated search is not a completeness certificate. Needed by: `CA.5/certified-number-field-output-interpretation`.
- **DiophantineApproximationAndTranscendence:DT.0** — Siegel's lemma over a number field with rational-integer unknowns in product form (Berrevoets, Lemma 3.2, printed p. 11): let K be a number field of degree d with complex embeddings σ₁, …, σ_d, let N > dM, and let a_ij ∈ 𝓞_K (1 ≤ i ≤ M, 1 ≤ j ≤ N) with every row i containing a nonzero a_ij. Then Σ_j a_ij x_j = 0 (1 ≤ i ≤ M) has a solution x ∈ ℤ^N ∖ {0} with max_j |x_j| ≤ ((2√2N)^{dM} ∏_{i=1}^{M} ∏_{k=1}^{d} max_j |σ_k(a_ij)|)^{1/(N−dM)}. DT.0/siegel-lemma-number-field bounds the solution through a uniform bound A on the houses, which loses the product over the embeddings (it gives house(α)^{dN} where Dobrowolski's argument needs M(α)^N); the product form is the generalisation that node's proof gives with the same pigeonhole argument. Needed by: `CA.6/dobrowolski-auxiliary-polynomial`.
- **AnalyticNumberTheory:AN.2** — The explicit Chebyshev-type count of primes in dyadic intervals (Rosser and Schoenfeld, Approximate formulas for some functions of prime numbers, Illinois J. Math. 6 (1962), 64-94, Corollary 3): for every real x ≥ 41/2, π(2x) − π(x) > 3x/(5 log x), with π Mathlib's Nat.primeCounting. Mathlib's Chebyshev bounds (Chebyshev.theta_le_log4_mul_x, Chebyshev.theta_ge') do not give a positive lower bound for π(2x) − π(x). Needed by: `CA.6/exists-prime-power-not-root`.
- **ArithmeticDynamics:DY.1** — The canonical height of a polarised endomorphism of P¹ over Q̄, normalised against Mathlib's absolute logarithmic Weil height: for f : P¹ → P¹ of degree d ≥ 2 defined over a number field (f*O(1) ≅ O(d)) and h([x : 1]) = NumberField.absLogHeight₁ x, h([1 : 0]) = 0, the limit ĥ_f(P) = lim_{n→∞} d^{−n} h(fⁿ(P)) exists for every P ∈ P¹(Q̄), |ĥ_f − h| is bounded, ĥ_f ∘ f = d·ĥ_f, and ĥ_f is the unique function with these two properties. Needed by: `CA.6/canonical-height-power-map`.
- **tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/NumberFieldArithmetic#layer-5-the-global-local-dictionary-at-finite-places** — For a finite Galois extension L/K of number fields and a nonzero prime v of 𝓞_K: the integral semilocal equivalence v.adicCompletionIntegers K ⊗[𝓞 K] 𝓞 L ≃ Π_{w|v} w.adicCompletionIntegers L with its pure-tensor formula (Layer 5.7), its equivariance for the permutation action of Gal(L/K) on the factors, the identification of the stabiliser of w with Gal(L_w/K_v) through decompositionHom (Layer 5.6), and the equality of the local ramification index with w.asIdeal.ramificationIdx (𝓞 K) (Layer 5.5). Needed by: `CA.7/completed-ring-of-integers-is-induced`, `CA.7/locally-free-of-tame`, `CA.7/ring-of-integers-locally-free-over-integral-group-ring`.
- **tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-2-unramified-extensions-and-frobenius** — For a finite extension L/K of nonarchimedean local fields: the unramified predicate and the residue correspondence Gal(L/K) ≃ Gal(𝓀_L/𝓀_K) for unramified Galois L/K (with p𝒪_L the maximal ideal); existence of the unramified extension of degree f as K(μ_{q^f−1}); composita of unramified extensions are unramified; the maximal unramified subextension L_0 with [L_0 : K] = f. Needed by: `CA.7/local-unramified-normal-integral-basis`, `CA.7/local-tame-uniformiser`, `CA.7/local-tame-split-extension`.
- **tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/LocalFieldsRamification#layer-3-ramification-the-tame-and-wild-cases-and-the-filtration** — For nonarchimedean local fields: the predicate IsTamelyRamified; a finite totally and tamely ramified L/K of degree e is K(α) with α^e a uniformiser of K; such L/K is Galois exactly when μ_e ⊆ K, and then Gal(L/K) ↪ μ_e by σ ↦ σ(α)/α; an Eisenstein polynomial X^e − π_K defines a totally ramified extension in which its root is a uniformiser; in a totally ramified extension 𝒪_L = 𝒪_K[π_L]; stability of tameness in towers. Needed by: `CA.7/local-totally-tamely-ramified-normal-integral-basis`, `CA.7/local-tame-uniformiser`, `CA.7/local-tame-split-extension`.
- **tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/ClassFieldTheory#layer-13-norm-theorems-and-class-fields** — kroneckerWeber: every finite abelian extension K/ℚ (a number field K with IsAbelianGalois ℚ K) admits a ℚ-algebra embedding K → CyclotomicField n ℚ for some n ≥ 1. Needed by: `CA.7/tame-abelian-field-in-squarefree-cyclotomic-field`.
- **tauceti:TauCetiRoadmap/RepresentationTheory/CharacterTheory#layer-7-the-frobenius-schur-indicator-and-realquaternionic-type** — The Frobenius–Schur trichotomy for irreducible complex representations of a finite group (indicator −1 exactly when there is a nonzero invariant alternating form: frobeniusSchurIndicatorRep_eq_neg_one_iff), and the count of real-valued irreducible characters by real conjugacy classes, giving that a group of odd order has no irreducible representation of quaternionic (symplectic) type. Needed by: `CA.7/taylor-no-symplectic-stably-free`, `CA.7/taylor-odd-order-free`.
- **KTheoryLowDegrees:Z.1** — Ring K₀ for an arbitrary (noncommutative) ring Λ as TauCeti.SplitK0 of TauCeti.finiteProjectiveModules Λ, with: the class [P] of a finitely generated projective module; [P] = [Q] exactly when P ⊕ Λ^k ≅ Q ⊕ Λ^k for some k (stable equality criterion); scalar extension along a ring homomorphism Λ → Λ′, [P] ↦ [Λ′ ⊗_Λ P]; and restriction of scalars along a ring homomorphism Λ → Λ′ that makes Λ′ finitely generated and projective as a left Λ-module (as for ℤ[G] → 𝓞_K[G]). Needed by: `CA.7/locally-free-class-group`.

## Structural proposals

Recorded in the packet's `restructure` list. This packet works with the current structure.

### `rescope`: ClassicalArithmeticCompletion, DiophantineApproximationAndTranscendence

Dimitrov's proof of the Schinzel-Zassenhaus conjecture (CA.6/schinzel-zassenhaus-dimitrov) needs the transfinite diameter (logarithmic capacity) of compact subsets of ℂ, Dubinin's bound for hedgehogs and the Pólya-Carlson-Bertrandias rationality theorem for integer power series. No roadmap of the atlas plans potential theory in the plane or arithmetic rationality criteria; DT.5 (E/G-functions) is the nearest, and André's G-function theory contains the same rationality criteria (the source notes that André's theorem VIII 1.6 can replace Bertrandias's).

**Proposal.** Add to DiophantineApproximationAndTranscendence:DT.5 the targets 'transfinite diameter of a compact subset of ℂ with its monotonicity and the value for segments and rotationally symmetric hedgehogs; Dubinin's hedgehog bound; the Pólya-Carlson-Bertrandias rationality theorem for power series in 𝓞_{F,S}[[1/X]]', consumed by ClassicalArithmeticCompletion:CA.6; or create a small roadmap 'Arithmetic algebraization and capacity' with these three targets, with DT.5 and CA.6 as consumers.

### `rescope`: ClassicalArithmeticCompletion

CA.7 is kept in this roadmap, as the accepted RS-03 decides, but it is too broad to read as one star: it now has 55 nodes in four coherent groups that share few prerequisites with each other, and none with CA.0–CA.6. Divide it into four sub-layers, each a parent node of the stage.

**Proposal.** CA.7a Normal bases, tameness and the trace: ring-of-integers-as-group-ring-module, rational-normal-basis-map, normal-basis-generators-differ-by-units, normal-integral-basis, trace-of-normal-integral-basis-generator, trace-surjective-of-normal-integral-basis, tamely-ramified, trace-modulo-a-prime, prime-divides-trace-ideal-iff-not-tame, tame-iff-trace-surjective, tame-of-normal-integral-basis, gaussian-integers-have-no-normal-integral-basis, rim-projectivity-criterion, projective-of-tame, trace-surjective-of-projective. CA.7b Noether's theorem (local and global): local-unramified-normal-integral-basis, local-totally-tamely-ramified-normal-integral-basis, local-tame-uniformiser, local-tame-split-extension, normal-integral-basis-of-split-extension, local-noether, locally-free-lattice, locally-free-lattice-is-projective, sublattice-eq-of-completions, completed-ring-of-integers-is-induced, locally-free-of-tame, noether-theorem, ring-of-integers-locally-free-over-integral-group-ring, locally-free-not-free-example. CA.7c Hilbert–Speiser and resolvents: normal-integral-basis-of-arithmetically-disjoint-compositum, prime-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis, squarefree-cyclotomic-normal-integral-basis, gauss-period-normal-integral-basis, tame-abelian-field-in-squarefree-cyclotomic-field, hilbert-speiser, lagrange-resolvent, group-determinant-factorisation, discriminant-of-normal-basis-resolvent-formula, normal-integral-basis-discriminant-criterion. CA.7d Orders and class groups: order-in-finite-dimensional-algebra, left-order-of-lattice, maximal-order, existence-of-maximal-orders, maximal-order-of-cyclic-group-algebra, associated-order, associated-order-of-free, associated-order-eq-group-ring-iff-tame, associated-order-of-gaussian-integers, locally-free-class-group, locally-free-class-represented-by-rank-one, ring-of-integers-class, froehlich-kernel-group, taylor-class-of-order-two, taylor-no-symplectic-stably-free, taylor-odd-order-free.

### `split`: ClassicalArithmeticCompletion, IntegralGaloisModuleStructure

Corrected form of the earlier proposal to move CA.7 out. Its first reason, that no free source existed and the layer could never be closed, no longer holds: the layer is planned from free sources and is partial only through three named gaps. Its second reason stands and is sharper now that the layer is planned: no node of CA.7 uses any node of CA.0–CA.6 or of CA.5, and every input of CA.7 comes from NumberFieldArithmetic, LocalFieldsRamification, ClassFieldTheory, CharacterTheory, GrothendieckEulerForms and KTheoryLowDegrees; its consumers (the Fröhlich–Taylor direction, Hilbert–Speiser fields, the ComputationalNumberTheory CN.2 algorithms of Bley–Johnston and Acciaro) are likewise outside this roadmap. The accepted RS-03 keeps CA.7 in place, so the split is recorded for the orchestrator's next restructuring round and the layer is planned where it stands.

**Proposal.** A roadmap IntegralGaloisModuleStructure (area of algebraic number theory) with the four sub-layers above as its layers G.0–G.3, prerequisites NumberFieldArithmetic, LocalFieldsRamification, ClassFieldTheory (Layer 13), RepresentationTheory/CharacterTheory (Layer 7) and KTheoryLowDegrees (Z.1, Z.4), and a fifth layer for the gaps it inherits (Fröhlich's Hom-description, root number classes, Galois Gauss sums, Jacobinski cancellation, Leopoldt's theorem on associated orders). ClassicalArithmeticCompletion would keep CA.0–CA.6 and lose the link CN.2 → CA.7.
